# API Integration and Publication Consistency

**Publication edition:** 1.1 · **Interface inventory date:** 4 November 2026

## 1. Purpose

The repository publishes knowledge and specifications. The website renders a selected immutable publication. The service exposes machine interfaces. These are related products with separate versions and evidence. A successful website build does not establish service deployment, a current search index, or admission of a complete task-specific Knowledge Contract.

This appendix defines the connection between those boundaries. The [core specification](../../engineering/specifications/knowledge-contract-core/SPECIFICATION.md) supplies the obligations for a ready contract; the [context-package specification](../../engineering/specifications/context-package/SPECIFICATION.md) supplies composition and delivery requirements.

Sections 3–7 define requirements using [BCP 14](../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). Section 2 is an informative inventory of the documented read surface, not a claim that a task-contract service or the receipt profile below has been deployed. Requirement identifiers remain stable within this appendix.

**API-R001.** Conformance MUST name the implemented profile, exact implementation revision and assessed requirements; publication of these requirements does not establish that conformance.

## 2. Documented public interface

The public site describes the following read surface:

| Interface | Documented role | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `https://api.contract.place/v1/search` | Retrieve candidate passages; used by the site's anonymous GET search | Retrieval ranking does not establish correctness, completeness or task admission |
| `https://api.contract.place/v1/related` | Retrieve related records | A relation or similarity is not an equivalence or authority grant |
| `https://api.contract.place/v1/fetch` | Fetch an addressed record through the read interface | The returned revision and representation must be inspected |
| `https://api.contract.place/mcp` | MCP access to the documented service surface | Availability of a tool does not establish every operation described by the research |
| `https://api.contract.place/health` | Service health information | Health is distinct from corpus freshness and scientific validity |

The existing site's `llms.txt` names the read schema `ContractPlaceRead/v1`. This appendix does not infer HTTP methods, parameter schemas or authentication behavior for routes other than the observed site search. Clients use the service's actual API contract. A task-specific contract delivery capability requires its own implemented and tested interface; no `/compose` endpoint is established here.

## 3. Contract delivery requirements

**API-R002.** **Request binding.** A service offering a ready Knowledge Contract MUST bind the request to the task and requirements revision, intended recipient and use, applicable constraints, required profiles, temporal mode, and resource limits.

**API-R003.** Where any required value is unknown, it MUST report the gap or obtain the missing information before asserting admission for that use.

**API-R004.** **Response and outcome.** The response MUST identify the exact contract and representation versions, selected knowledge revisions, sources and grounds, mandatory dependency closure, coverage, omitted or unresolved obligations, applicable constraints, assessments and the decision's trust boundary.

**API-R005.** Its operation outcome MUST be one of **`success`, `partial`, `refused`, `failed`, `unresolved`**, with the [status model's meanings](../../engineering/specifications/status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies).

**API-R006.** `ready` is an availability or lifecycle state; it MUST NOT replace an outcome token.

A failure to execute the delivery operation is `failed`; an outcome or material obligation that cannot be established is `unresolved`.

**API-R007.** **Admission scope.** A ready-for-use assertion MUST be backed by the mandatory checks for the declared use.

A nonempty JSON object or successful search is insufficient. A response that supports reading may still lack the capability or grant needed for execution. A rendered skill description cannot supply those missing facts.

**API-R008.** The **Knowledge Contract admission runtime** is the service boundary responsible for such a decision; its actual implementation and profile versions MUST be identified rather than inferred from the publication edition or the legacy field `kc2_admission`.

**API-R009.** **Execution binding.** If the contract is issued for execution, the system MUST subsequently bind the actual attempt, actor, environment, contract revision, outputs, effects and acceptance evidence.

Knowledge delivery and execution acceptance are separate operations.

## 4. Three manifests

Publication consistency requires three independently inspectable identities:

| Manifest | Required identity |
| --- | --- |
| Repository publication catalogue | Edition and the canonical document paths/types in one source commit |
| Website publication manifest | Exact source repository commit, edition, document hashes, renderer version and generated routes |
| API index manifest | Actual indexed source commits and content hashes, build identity, admitted paths and applicable decisions |

**Website identity.** The website's source commit is pinned in `publication-manifest.json` in the site repository. Its generated `publication.json` binds reader routes to source hashes.

**API-R010.** A deployment MUST build those pages from the declared commit and reject an uncommitted or mismatched source tree.

**Repository-scoped comparison.** The API may contain multiple repositories or releases.

**API-R011.** A client MUST establish repository identity before comparing a result's source revision with the reader's publication commit.

The canonical repository identifier for this project is **`https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract`**; a revision comparison is scoped to this identifier.

**API-R012.** It MUST NOT be applied to an unrelated source.

**API-R013.** Unknown and different revisions MUST be distinguished from a match.

An API-provided label of “current” alone is not proof of a matching publication.

In the [inspected site adapter](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/contract-place-site/blob/cdca604245165bbec80ce3624fdc65e9cbde6a01/src/site/search.js), the relevant response fields are **`source_revision`** and **`citation.canonical_url`**. The latter supplies repository identity through an HTTPS GitHub URL of the form `https://github.com/{owner}/{repository}/blob/{full-commit}/{path}`; the adapter compares the identified owner/repository and revision with the pinned reader. The normalized `repository_url` used by the receipt profile below is an operator-record field, not a claim that the backend already returns a field with that name. If the canonical URL is absent, malformed, points to another host or does not identify an immutable revision, a matching publication cannot be established.

**API-R014.** A client MUST NOT substitute an untrusted title, branch label or bare hash for that identity.

**API-R015.** If URL revision and `source_revision` conflict, it MUST report a mismatch.

The read schema's published field contract, once inspected, remains authoritative for the actual API representation.

## 5. Observed mismatch and release gate

The pre-restructure site linked the newer paper entries while a search inspection returned a Knowledge Contract snapshot at `39e05969728ea7a51f162cdbefac32fd8f54dbb6`, whose README still described publication folders as empty. That was a consistency mismatch between the reading surface and search evidence, not proof that the newer documents were absent from GitHub.

The reader addresses the presentation boundary by exposing its exact source revision and showing whether a search result's revision matches it.

**API-R016.** Refreshing or promoting the backend index is a separate operation. **Admission governance.** An indexing operation MUST use the repository's existing publication-decision and allowlist process; a catalogue entry is not a substitute for that approval.

Publishing this appendix does not admit paths, refresh an index or change an allowlist.

**API-R017.** **Release gate.** Before a release is described as consistently indexed, the operator MUST record all six checks below against the exact source revision.

A health check alone cannot pass this gate. A missing observation is `unknown`, not `pass`.

| Check key | Required observation |
| --- | --- |
| `admitted_snapshot` | Admitted path hashes and decision references agree with the intended immutable source snapshot |
| `index_snapshot` | The actual index build and manifest identify that exported snapshot |
| `reported_revision` | Returned result identities report the intended repository and revision |
| `query_coverage` | A declared query set retrieves the specified expected documents; selection and exclusions are recorded |
| `supersession` | Removed or superseded records have the declared observed status, including an explicit empty inventory when none apply |
| `fetch_identity` | Fetching every retained result identity returns the same revision and content hash reported for that result |

### Release receipt profile

**API-R018.** **Receipt.** A gate record conforming to **`KCIndexReleaseReceipt/v1`** MUST satisfy the [receipt JSON Schema](index-release-receipt.schema.json) and the semantic checks here.

It is a new operator interchange profile for this publication, not evidence that the existing service emits it. This appendix supplies no production receipt and claims no new deployment or index run.

| Required field | Representation and meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `schema` | Exact token `KCIndexReleaseReceipt/v1` |
| `repository_url`, `source_revision` | Canonical HTTPS repository URL and full 40-character lowercase Git commit identity; one receipt covers one repository revision |
| `observed_at`, `observer`, `evidence_refs` | RFC 3339 observation time, accountable observer identity and durable evidence references for the actual run |
| `publication_manifest_sha256` | SHA-256 of the website/publication manifest used for comparison |
| `index_build_id`, `index_manifest_sha256` | Actual backend build identity and exact manifest hash, not a desired future value |
| `admitted_paths` | Nonempty list of `{path, sha256, decision_ref}` for the exact admitted source files |
| `queries` | Nonempty declared list of `{id, query, expected_paths, results}`; each result records `{record_id, path, source_revision, source_sha256, representation_sha256, canonical_url}` |
| `fetches` | Readback list of `{record_id, source_revision, representation_sha256}` for every retained query result |
| `superseded_records` | List of `{record_id, expected_status, observed_status, evidence_ref}`; `[]` explicitly means that the inspected change inventory has no such records |
| `checks` | All six named gate outcomes, each `pass`, `fail` or `unknown`; no omitted check |
| `decision` | `pass` only if every check passed; otherwise `fail` if any check failed, and `unknown` when none failed but at least one is unknown |
| `scope`, `limitations` | The query selection rationale, included/excluded paths and limits of observation; limitations may be an explicitly empty list |

**API-R019.** Query IDs, admitted paths, and record IDs within each results or fetch list MUST be unique.

**API-R020.** The same record MAY be found by several different queries.

**API-R021.** Each query's expected paths MUST be drawn from `admitted_paths`; every expected path MUST have a matching result before `query_coverage` can pass.

**API-R022.** Each retained result MUST identify the declared repository and `source_revision`, and its source path and `source_sha256` MUST match the admitted snapshot.

`representation_sha256` identifies the exact returned representation bytes, separately from the whole source file.

**API-R023.** A service returning passages MUST provide both bindings or leave the relevant check unknown.

**API-R024.** Every result MUST have a matching fetch identity and representation hash before `fetch_identity` can pass.

**API-R025.** Every superseded record's observed status MUST equal its required status before `supersession` can pass.

Schema validation alone does not establish these correspondences, the truth of recorded observations or authority to index.

## 6. Client presentation

**API-R026.** **Presentation.** The default result view SHOULD show the passage, human title, source link and indexed revision.

**API-R027.** Technical evidence about admission, uncertainty and score semantics MAY be placed in an accessible details section.

**API-R028.** Material limits MUST remain visible where they change a user's decision.

**API-R029.** A retrieval score MUST NOT be presented as a probability that a claim is true without an independently established calibration and its scope.

**API-R030.** Scientific reading SHOULD remain possible without making API calls: full pages, equations, references and evidence links are served as the static selected edition.

API failure therefore need not remove the published scientific argument.

## 7. Acceptance cases

**API-R031.** **Acceptance cases.** The integration MUST reject a site build from the wrong source commit, a dirty publication tree, a missing referenced document and a local link escaping the publication.

**API-R032.** Search presentation MUST distinguish a matching Knowledge Contract revision, an older revision, an unknown revision and a record from another repository.

**API-R033.** Rendering MUST preserve equations, tables, footnotes and document anchors.

**API-R034.** Receipt validation MUST reject omitted checks, a passing decision with a nonpassing check, an ambiguous repository/revision identity, duplicate identities, missing expected paths, and a mismatched fetch readback.

**API-R035.** A contract-delivery acceptance suite MUST additionally exercise missing constraints, a mandatory unknown profile, incomplete closure, insufficient budget, revoked disclosure authority, stale source evidence, and a correct informational response that does not authorize execution.

No successful result is claimed here for an interface that has not undergone that suite.

For the evidential interpretation of these checks, see [evidence and falsification](../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md), [controlled reuse](../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md), and the [engineering studies](../../engineering/studies/README.md).

# Source Map, Earlier Editions, and Protocol Compatibility

**Publication edition:** 1.1 · **Source audit date:** 4 November 2026

## 1. What this edition changes

The earlier repository contained fourteen short English paper entry pages, fourteen much longer Russian manuscripts, ten English specification entries and ten Russian specification texts. The long texts combined scientific arguments, domain studies, iteration notes and references to supporting records outside the public release.

This edition reorganizes those materials into eight complete English scientific papers, eleven engineering specifications, six engineering studies, a common glossary and bibliography, and two supporting appendices. Scientific results, normative requirements and observations are now separately identifiable. The prior iteration histories remain recoverable at an immutable repository revision instead of appearing as repeated handoff sections in the current reading path.

The exact prior publication snapshot is [b50243b28e9ad7376e28c749a21479ef46ec9d60](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract/tree/b50243b28e9ad7376e28c749a21479ef46ec9d60). The prior site snapshot is [64ddd981296cb65390beecd802f1682618a7f080](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/contract-place-site/tree/64ddd981296cb65390beecd802f1682618a7f080). These references are historical evidence, not aliases for the mutable main branches.

The independent review addressed published edition 1.0 at [ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract/tree/ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e). Corrections are made from that immutable base. Historical evidence and its origin hashes remain unchanged; corrected interpretations and new finite checks have their own records. The current documents identify the stronger premises needed by the formal results, the actual scope of the observations, and the breaking book-package profile described below.

## 2. Scientific crosswalk

| Earlier manuscript | Current scientific home | Material separated into engineering |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Introduction | [Semantic objects and context](../../papers/semantic-objects-and-context/PAPER.md) | [Publication inventory](../../publication-catalog.json) and [API interface boundaries](../api-integration/APPENDIX.md#2-documented-public-interface); no inventory establishes implementation conformance |
| Semantic objects and extraction | [Semantic objects and context](../../papers/semantic-objects-and-context/PAPER.md) | [Source transcription and uncertainty](../../engineering/studies/source-transcription-and-uncertainty/STUDY.md) |
| Context and applicability | [Semantic objects and context](../../papers/semantic-objects-and-context/PAPER.md) | [Declared dependency graphs and finite delivery checks](../../engineering/studies/dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md) |
| Transformations and transfer | [Constrained transformations](../../papers/constrained-transformations/PAPER.md) | [Executable projections and revision](../../engineering/studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md) |
| Query, retrieval and coverage | [Retrieval and context composition](../../papers/retrieval-and-context-composition/PAPER.md) | [Coverage and delivery requirements](../../engineering/specifications/context-package/SPECIFICATION.md); no finite retrieval-search performance study is claimed |
| Budgeted context composition | [Retrieval and context composition](../../papers/retrieval-and-context-composition/PAPER.md) | [Delivered record counts and fault controls](../../engineering/studies/dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md#delivered-record-counts-and-fault-controls); these are graph records, not measured token or byte costs |
| Dialogue and learning | [Interaction and memory](../../papers/interaction-and-memory/PAPER.md) | [Operation and task bindings](../../engineering/specifications/contract-family/SPECIFICATION.md#2-common-contract-record) and [targeted revision cases](../../engineering/studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md#targeted-revision-and-a-strong-alternative); no longitudinal dialogue or learning study |
| Reasoning and evidence | [Evidence and falsification](../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md) | [Checker trust boundaries and counterexamples](../../engineering/studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md#counterexamples-and-the-comparator) |
| Documents and memory | [Interaction and memory](../../papers/interaction-and-memory/PAPER.md) | [Readable projection audit](../../engineering/studies/source-transcription-and-uncertainty/STUDY.md) and [single-section editorial pilot](../../engineering/studies/editorial-skill-pilot/STUDY.md) |
| Work, roles, skills and Knowledge Contract | [Controlled knowledge reuse](../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md) and [revision and governance](../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md) | [Core](../../engineering/specifications/knowledge-contract-core/SPECIFICATION.md), [artifact registry](../../engineering/specifications/artifact-registry/SPECIFICATION.md) and [profile conformance](../../engineering/studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md) |
| Revision and canon | [Revision and governance](../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md) | [Versioned-rule requirements](../../engineering/specifications/knowledge-evolution/SPECIFICATION.md) and [bounded addressed revision observations](../../engineering/studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md#targeted-revision-and-a-strong-alternative) |
| Evaluation and falsification | [Evidence and falsification](../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md) | [Claim–evidence index](../../engineering/studies/evidence-index.json), [ordinary versus boundary counts](../../engineering/studies/README.md#counting-and-interpretation), and [comparator tie](../../engineering/studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md#counterexamples-and-the-comparator) |
| Compilation, caching and reuse | [Controlled knowledge reuse](../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md) | [Compiled-artifact requirements](../../engineering/specifications/compiled-artifact-contract/SPECIFICATION.md), [invalidation](../../engineering/specifications/cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md) and [reuse policy](../../engineering/specifications/reuse-policy/SPECIFICATION.md); no deployed generation or idempotence experiment |
| Knowledge utility and resource economics | [Knowledge utility](../../papers/knowledge-utility/PAPER.md) | [Synthetic admission checks and analytical costs](../../engineering/studies/reuse-admission-and-cost-controls/STUDY.md); no measured production cost benefit |

The numerical filenames of the earlier edition are retained only as compatibility links. They lead to the current scientific home and to their exact earlier English and Russian texts. Consolidation is topical: it does not silently convert an untested historical proposal into a proved claim. A historical claim not supported by the present argument or evidence remains a historical proposal, accessible in the source snapshot.

## 3. Engineering crosswalk

The ten earlier specification subjects map one-to-one to these current files:

| Earlier subject | Current specification |
| --- | --- |
| Knowledge Contract v3 | [Core model and admission](../../engineering/specifications/knowledge-contract-core/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Contract family | [Knowledge operations](../../engineering/specifications/contract-family/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Semantic types | [Semantic types and representations](../../engineering/specifications/semantic-types/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Context package | [Coverage, dependencies and delivery](../../engineering/specifications/context-package/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Compiled artifact contract | [Compiled artifacts and derivation](../../engineering/specifications/compiled-artifact-contract/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Artifact registry | [Readable names and document formats](../../engineering/specifications/artifact-registry/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Status model | [Assessment, execution and publication states](../../engineering/specifications/status-model/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Cache identity and invalidation | [Identity and invalidation](../../engineering/specifications/cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Reuse policy | [Time, authorization and outcomes](../../engineering/specifications/reuse-policy/SPECIFICATION.md) |
| Knowledge evolution contract | [Versioned rules](../../engineering/specifications/knowledge-evolution/SPECIFICATION.md) |

The additional [book-package specification](../../engineering/specifications/book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) originated with the schema, validator and synthetic fixtures added in upstream revision [570f76b6d9a189295c3229254821c30748383965](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract/tree/570f76b6d9a189295c3229254821c30748383965). Edition 1.0 preserved those upstream bytes. The current correction defines the breaking **`book-package/2`** profile and **`KCBook*/v2`** records: review results bind to their actual subject, scope and criteria; rights classification constrains permissible licensing and export; readiness follows the complete declared state rules. This is an explicit format change, not a silent reinterpretation of historical `v1` records.

The last reviewed `v1` implementation remains available at [ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract/tree/ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e). A consumer MUST identify the profile and schema versions it accepts. Conversion to `v2` requires a new identified record and any missing assessment evidence; renaming a schema token MUST NOT turn an old review, rights assertion or status into a newly established fact. No automatic reassessment or corpus-admission change is performed by publishing these documents.

Engineering studies contain the practical domain material, including the historical cooking, navigation and electrical examples. They distinguish publicly replayable procedures from inspectable retained reports and unavailable third-party source corpora. The [evidence index](../../engineering/studies/evidence-index.json) records claim-level support and limits; a navigational link alone is not an empirical justification.

## 4. Protocol compatibility

Publication edition **1.0** identifies the original reviewed set of documents; the current publication's edition is stated in its catalogue and metadata. Neither is a renamed runtime protocol. The following identifiers are preserved where historical records need them. Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

| Identifier | Actual scope | Compatibility statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `3.0.0-draft.1` | Earlier target Knowledge Contract architecture | No stable protocol release is inferred from the editorial edition |
| `kc-synthesis/0.1` | Bounded interpreted-JSON core/profile implementation | Its retained results do not implement all requirements of the current architecture |
| `mm-envelope/0.1` | Common carrier used by the historical registry and examples | The carrier and its 34 artifact types are neither an exhaustive taxonomy of knowledge nor a limit on later contracts |
| `i16-compiled-artifact/0.1` | Bounded compiled-artifact representation | Original observations retain their original profile and validator scope |
| `i17-reuse-policy/0.1` | Bounded time and reuse-policy model | Interval calculations do not constitute production authorization or distributed atomic serving |
| `knowledge-evolution-metadata/0.2-proposal` | Earlier evolution metadata proposal | Publication of architectural requirements does not claim a deployed migration service |
| Knowledge Contract admission runtime | Service that decides admission under its explicitly advertised profiles and policies | No stable deployed runtime version is established by this publication; an operator MUST supply its actual implementation and profile identities when claiming conformance |
| `kc2_admission` | Legacy book-record field name | It is not a runtime or protocol version. Its presence does not establish admission, and the publication does not rename it to imply a new runtime |
| `KCBook*/v1` | Book records in the immutable edition-1.0 base | Historical schema behavior remains auditable at `ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e`; conversion does not retroactively validate it |
| `book-package/2`, `KCBook*/v2` | Current corrected book representation and validator profile | Breaking fields and decisions require explicit migration and fresh evidence where absent; they do not identify the admission runtime |

An implementation claiming this edition MUST state which requirements it satisfies and link corresponding evidence. A change of schema, field semantics or wire version requires its own migration and compatibility record. The publication process MUST NOT rewrite retained raw evidence merely to remove historical development labels.

## 5. Provenance and rights

Each study's `evidence/origin.json` identifies the origin, exact output hash and transformation status of its evidence files. An exact recovered JSON is distinguished from a readable prose view reconstructed from retained HTML. The latter does not claim to reproduce unavailable original Markdown bytes.

Third-party books, page images and OCR corpora are not redistributed here. The current arguments are self-contained; publicly runnable examples use original synthetic data or metadata-only graphs. Restricted source access limits independent reproduction of the corresponding historical experiments, and the studies state that limit at the claim it affects.

Project text remains [CC BY 4.0](../../LICENSE); code remains [Apache-2.0](../../LICENSE-CODE). The current [source register](../../SOURCES.md) replaces the earlier catch-all description for the new edition with named primary works and explicit document coverage.

## 6. Reading and citation

Cite a paper by its human-readable title, author, publication edition and immutable revision or released DOI when one actually exists. No DOI is invented for this edition. Use the paper for its argument, the specification for a requirement, and the study or exact evidence record for an observation. The [glossary](../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md) resolves shared terms without repeating the full definitions in every article.

## 7. Internal review and verification

The [publication review](../../reviews/publication-integrity/REVIEW.md) records the actual scientific and evidence reviews, exact input identities, integration corrections, local checks and unperformed release checks. It distinguishes internal content acceptance from external peer review and from the separate repository, website and corpus-admission outcomes.

BLOB engineering/specifications/artifact-registry/SPECIFICATION.md ec6584f97c5160fd3c8b17a5036aaeb8d302bf86dd61e567f2c5d858e9159470 original 8193

title: "Artifact Registry, Readable Names, and Document Formats"
artifact: "engineering-specification"

# Artifact Registry, Readable Names, and Document Formats

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

The registry gives humans and agents a predictable way to find, interpret and revise artifacts. It separates the identity of an object from its title, revision, file representation and current assessment. A new technical term does not automatically require a new database table or service.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). The formats below are project conventions except where an external format is explicitly named. They do not claim to be universal standards for competencies, roles or blueprints.

## 2. Names, identity and revisions

**REG-R001.** Every current artifact MUST live in a uniquely named directory within its type collection.

**REG-R002.** Directory names use meaningful lowercase words separated by single hyphens; a reader MUST be able to infer the topic without looking up an internal number.

Names such as `r1`, `r10` or `p09` are not valid titles or current canonical slugs.

**REG-R003.** Historical identifiers MAY remain in provenance and migration maps.

The main file has a predictable name determined by type. A rename preserves provenance and provides a compatibility map when an old path was published. An immutable revision identifies a particular content state. A mutable “current” pointer is a selection governed by a separate decision, not a new identity for every request.

## 3. Publication formats

| Collection | Main file | Required content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `papers/<topic>/` | `PAPER.md` | Abstract, question and contribution, definitions, argument or results, counterexamples, limitations, references to engineering evidence |
| `engineering/specifications/<topic>/` | `SPECIFICATION.md` | Purpose, conformance, model, requirements, failure handling, acceptance cases, compatibility, evidence |
| `engineering/studies/<topic>/` | `STUDY.md` | Question, materials and rights, method, actual results, adverse cases, reproducibility, limitations, links to claims |
| `reference/glossary/` | `GLOSSARY.md` | Preferred terms, definitions, distinctions, aliases and language mapping |
| `reference/bibliography/` | `BIBLIOGRAPHY.md` | Identified works, primary URLs or DOIs, and the role of each source |
| `appendices/<topic>/` | `APPENDIX.md` | A named supporting subject, its scope, references and relationship to the main documents |

**REG-R004.** Current publications MUST appear in `publication-catalog.json`.

The catalogue records type, slug, path, title, summary, language and edition. It is a reading manifest, not an authorization to index or execute the content.

**REG-R005.** Each main Markdown file MUST have YAML front matter with `name`, `title`, `artifact`, `edition`, `author`, `language`, `license`, and a nonempty `description`.

Sources are registered through the repository source register.

**REG-R006.** It MUST contain one descriptive H1.

**REG-R007.** Version and machine identifiers belong in metadata, compatibility notes or provenance; they MUST NOT dominate the reading title.

Relative links connect the current edition. Historical claims link to pinned revisions.

## 4. Constraints and professional artifacts

The professional profile extends the same folder convention. Collections are created when actual artifacts exist; empty speculative directories are unnecessary.

| Artifact | Main file | Meaning and required sections |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Role | `ROLE.md` | Responsibility; scope; permitted decisions; required skills or competencies; interfaces; accountability |
| Skill | `SKILL.md` | A reusable procedure; when to use it; inputs; steps; checks; constraints; outputs; supporting resources |
| Competency | `COMPETENCY.md` | An ability requirement or assessed claim; subject; performance criteria; assessed conditions; evidence; validity limits |
| Blueprint | `BLUEPRINT.md` | A solution structure or process; intended result; components or steps; dependencies; choices; constraints; acceptance |
| Constraint | `CONSTRAINT.md` | Subject; scope; normative rule or condition; origin/authority; enforcement/check; exceptions; failure behavior |
| Work Item | `WORK_ITEM.md` | Goal; requirements revision; inputs; assignments; constraints; plan; result criteria; attempts and outcomes |

A competency definition says what ability would count. A competency assessment binds evidence to a particular executor and conditions. Neither a role nor a skill file certifies that ability. A blueprint organizes work; it does not supply authorization. Constraints are linked directly from all artifacts to which they apply, not buried in a general note or mistaken for optional advice.

For skills, the external [Agent Skills specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) governs the `SKILL.md` filename and required `name` and `description` front matter. This project uses its supported `metadata` map for extra project fields rather than inventing incompatible required top-level keys. The skill name matches its directory. Supporting code, references and assets remain inside that skill's directory. The document-body headings above are a project convention, not mandatory headings imposed by Agent Skills.

Repository instructions use [AGENTS.md](https://agents.md/), a plain Markdown instruction file. It is not a universal serialization format for a role or an agent identity.

**REG-R008.** Provider-specific executable agent configurations MUST have an explicit adapter and remain distinct from the human-readable role definition.

## 5. Registry obligations

**REG-R009.** Each registered revision MUST identify its kind, representation profile, identity, revision, license or disclosure restriction, provenance, required dependencies and applicable observations.

**REG-R010.** A reader MUST distinguish known, unknown, not applicable and withheld information.

**REG-R011.** Required references MUST resolve to the intended revision or report an unresolved dependency.

Historical registration of 34 artifact types describes one example registry; it does not establish 34 exhaustive kinds of knowledge. Its common envelope is a carrier for those examples, not a restriction on all future contracts. The [source map](../../../appendices/source-map/APPENDIX.md) preserves that compatibility context outside the scientific argument.

## 6. Acceptance and migration

**REG-R012.** Registry checks MUST reject duplicate canonical names, missing required main files, incompatible front matter, broken current links and silent identity reuse.

**REG-R013.** A rename test MUST show where the previous path leads and how a cited immutable revision remains recoverable.

Unknown required extensions block use; preserving unknown optional data does not establish that it was interpreted.

**REG-R014.** Migration MUST preserve source identity and original revisions, record the mapping, and disclose fields that could not be translated.

**REG-R015.** It MUST NOT infer a capability, grant or successful execution from the presence of an artifact.

The [revision paper](../../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md) explains these distinctions; the [editorial study](../../studies/editorial-skill-pilot/STUDY.md) records an actual bounded use of skills and a blueprint without claiming superiority from their existence.

BLOB engineering/specifications/book-package/SPECIFICATION.md 1ce2ce4fa9e0e2647b3472f1da0a250fa3abae00c78b7518d3497f106c09722c original 22469

name: "book-package"
title: "Book Packages: Source Identity, Semantic Layers, and Validation"
artifact: "engineering-specification"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"
license: "CC-BY-4.0"

# Book Packages: Source Identity, Semantic Layers, and Validation

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

| Implementation component | Scope |
| --- | --- |
| [Schema](../../../schemas/book-package.schema.json) | `book-package/2`, five `KCBook*/v2` record families |
| [Validator and projection](../../../tools/check_book_package.py) | Structural and cross-file checks, safe Markdown rendering, candidate rows |
| [Builder](../../../tools/build_book_package.py) | Wraps source bytes and supplied conversion output; does not interpret a book |
| [Synthetic fixtures](../../../schemas/fixtures/book-package/) | Invented source and negative records; no third-party book text |
| [Conformance tests](../../../tools/test_book_package.py) · [review regressions](../../../tools/test_book_package_review.py) | Validation, reversible edits, rights, scoped review, rendering and status derivation |

## 1. Purpose and validation boundary

A book package binds one source representation to its conversion, semantic records and review observations. This specification defines the implemented `book-package/2` profile. Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). Record validation does not establish conversion fidelity, semantic truth, actual reviewer independence, publication rights or task-specific admission.

**BPK-R001.** The manifest MUST declare `validation_profile: book-package/2`, and every record MUST use its `KCBookPackageManifest/v2`, `KCBookObject/v2`, `KCBookRelation/v2`, `KCBookEdit/v2` or `KCBookQuality/v2` tag.

Unknown versions are unsupported.

**BPK-R002.** A consumer MUST NOT silently reinterpret a version 1 record as version 2.

The **Knowledge Contract admission runtime** decides use under the [core contract](../knowledge-contract-core/SPECIFICATION.md). The inherited literal field `kc2_admission` is not a protocol version.

**BPK-R003.** Its value in producer records MUST remain `not_measured`; the producer cannot grant admission.

`not_measured` maps to assessment `unknown` with reason “no observation,” never to `pass`.

## 2. Layout and reversible edits

```text
<package_id>/
  manifest.json
  document.md
  readable.md
  assets/
  edits.jsonl
  objects.jsonl
  relations.jsonl
  quality.json
```

**BPK-R004.** The package identifier MUST be a readable lowercase hyphenated slug, 3–96 characters; a single letter followed by digits is forbidden.

`release_id` identifies the particular release separately.

**BPK-R005.** Component paths MUST remain relative and inside the package, with no parent traversal or symlinks.

`document.md` is the unchanged converter output.

**BPK-R006.** Accepted edits MUST target `readable_md`; `asset_text` has no supported digest-chain semantics and is rejected.

**BPK-R007.** A package without `document_md` and `readable_md` MUST NOT contain accepted edits or a built semantic layer.

**BPK-R008.** Each accepted edit MUST have a nonempty original and replacement, an anchor, basis, reason, actor, input digest and output digest.

**BPK-R009.** At its step, the original MUST occur exactly once, and the replacement MUST occur exactly once in the resulting text.

Occurrences include overlaps: `aa` occurs twice in `xaaay`.

**BPK-R010.** Applying the accepted edits in ledger order MUST produce `readable.md`; undoing them in reverse order MUST reproduce `document.md` exactly.

Insertions, deletions and ambiguous repeated-fragment edits are unsupported by this search-reversible profile.

**BPK-R011.** JSON readers MUST reject duplicate object member names at every depth, non-finite number tokens and a UTF-8 BOM, so independent readers cannot choose different rights or status values from the same record.

**BPK-R012.** All comparison and hashing MUST use exact bytes with strict UTF-8 decoding and no newline translation.

`\r\n`, `\r` and `\n` remain distinct. JSON Lines, `SHA256SUMS` and check lists split only on `\n`; one final newline is allowed, blank JSON Lines and lines ending in `\r` are rejected. U+2028, U+0085 and other control characters do not become additional record separators.

The edit lifecycle is `pending` → `accepted` or `rejected`; `stale` means an obsolete proposal whose base no longer matches, and is never replayed. Accepted ledger entries are immutable: a later correction creates another entry or a new package release. A stale proposal's declared or overlapping dependents remain unresolved until reassessed in a new valid record.

## 3. Identity and integrity observations

`work_id` identifies the work, `edition_id` the edition and language, and `representation_id` one source file. Translations and editions retain separate identities and anchors, with explicit `related_works` relations. A representation records its source digest, size, format, integrity (`verified`, `mismatch`, `unverified`) and completeness (`full`, `partial`, `bibliography_only`).

**BPK-R013.** A `verified` or `mismatch` integrity declaration MUST include `integrity_check` with `method`, `observer`, `checked_at`, `expected_sha256` and `observed_sha256`.

**BPK-R014.** The observed digest MUST equal the representation digest.

Verified integrity requires equal expected and observed digests; mismatch requires different digests.

**BPK-R015.** The expected digest MUST NOT be replaced to conceal a mismatch.

This is an attributed producer observation. The validator checks its shape and internal consistency; it cannot authenticate the expected digest's authority or reconstruct an unavailable source. The builder records whether its expected digest was supplied by the caller.

**BPK-R016.** A production consumer MUST authenticate the relevant observation at its declared trust boundary before treating it as evidence of source integrity.

**BPK-R017.** `document_md.input_sha256` MUST equal the representation digest and its component record MUST name the converter and version.

With no supported converter output, the package remains `adapter_required`; no empty or invented document substitutes for conversion.

## 4. Semantic objects and facets

An object identifies a concept, claim, procedure, figure, table, formula, code block, example or other structure.

**BPK-R018.** Every object MUST retain its text, language, origin, at least one source anchor, status and all eight facet fields: `representation_modes`, `content_kinds`, `discourse_functions`, `aboutness`, `source_position`, `semantic_operators`, `assessment_refs`, and `governance_refs`.

Qualifiers carry conditions and numeric units when present.

The facets implement a bounded profile of [semantic types](../semantic-types/SPECIFICATION.md). Representation modes include `text`, `image`, `audio`, `video`, `structured_data` and their explicit combinations. Source positions are `asserts`, `hypothesizes`, `presumes`, `denies`, `asks`, `reports_other_position`: `presumes` denotes a presupposition and is not a synonym of hypothesis.

**BPK-R019.** Operators include epistemic and deontic modality, quantifier, negation, condition, exception and order; each operator MUST name its scope.

A free-text qualifier condition applies to the whole object unless a scoped operator supplies the more precise binding.

The nineteen content-kind tokens are `definition`, `state_description`, `observation_measurement`, `generalization`, `explanatory_model`, `formal_model`, `procedure`, `structure_configuration`, `constraint_norm`, `goal_intent`, `preference_evaluation`, `argument_evidence`, `example`, `counterexample`, `question_problem_gap`, `narrative_episode`, `analogy_metaphor`, `hypothesis_interpretation`, and `commitment_speech_act`. This vocabulary is not an exhaustive taxonomy.

**BPK-R020.** Each facet MUST be `known` with a value, or `unknown`, `not_applicable` or `withheld` with an explicit reason and no value.

Known representation modes, content kinds, discourse functions and aboutness are nonempty by this profile's cardinality rule. Known-empty operators, assessment references or governance references assert an established empty set and are allowed only with that meaning.

**BPK-R021.** An object named by a package review MUST include that review's identifier in known `assessment_refs`; known-empty cannot deny the very review that cites it.

Unknown references remain unknown rather than empty.

**BPK-R022.** A `benchmark` claim MUST additionally record corpus, device, tool version, settings, units and mode.

Structural presence does not verify those declarations or establish task applicability.

## 5. Relations

A relation connects two existing, different objects of the same package with one of `defines`, `uses`, `variant_of`, `requires`, `compared_with`, `contradicts`.

**BPK-R023.** Every relation MUST retain its conditions, origin, evidence, endpoints and status.

Every relation has at least one evidence anchor tagged `from`, `to` or `both`.

**BPK-R024.** A `contradicts` relation MUST include separate `from` and `to` evidence, so one-sided attribution cannot masquerade as a two-sided finding.

**BPK-R025.** A relation touching a stale or rejected endpoint MUST itself be stale or rejected.

Review of a relation does not silently rehabilitate its endpoint.

## 6. Evidence anchors and source formats

**BPK-R026.** Every anchor MUST bind the same representation identifier and source digest as its manifest.

The implemented format-to-anchor mapping is exact:

| Anchor kind | Exact format tokens | Coordinates |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `page_bbox` | `pdf`, `djvu`, `ps` | page from 1; bounding box with positive area; origin and unit |
| `spine_offset` | `epub` | member `spine_item`, optional element path, start/end offsets |
| `section_block` | `html`, `md`, `txt`, `tex` | section and block identifiers |
| `sheet_range` | `xlsx` | sheet, cell range, `formula` / `cached_value` / `literal` layer |
| `json_pointer` | `json` | RFC 6901 pointer |
| `slide_block` | `ppt`, `pptx` | slide number and block identifier |
| none | `zip`, `tar_gz`, `sevenz` | opaque source identity only; archive member addressing is unsupported |

**BPK-R027.** Archives MAY be retained as an unavailable source requiring an adapter, but MUST NOT carry converter output, edits or semantic records under this profile.

A member-path anchor requires a separately versioned extension. DocC is not a format token; a DocC JSON file uses `json`.

The validator checks geometry, ordering and the declared mapping, not whether a cited source location exists or supports the text. Extracting a different representation creates a new binding; it does not justify reusing coordinates from the previous one.

## 7. Attribution and safe rendering

Origins are `author_statement`, `editor_correction`, `generated_meaning`, `generated_description`, `our_experiment` and `model_inference`.

**BPK-R028.** A non-author origin MUST have a generation record and `presented_as_author: false`.

**BPK-R029.** An author statement MUST NOT carry a generation record.

On an author statement, `presented_as_author: true` means an asserted verbatim quotation; `false` means an explicitly attributed paraphrase. This flag never establishes quotation fidelity by itself.

**BPK-R030.** The public Markdown renderer MUST preserve every object and relation field, including qualifiers, operator scopes, source position, conditions, anchors, generation and assessment references.

It emits each complete record in a JSON literal block, with a fence longer than any backtick run in the payload. Origin labels and record headings are emitted outside that container from controlled fields.

**BPK-R031.** Payload headings, HTML or apparent origin labels MUST NOT create new rendered record structure.

Every non-author block is visibly labelled “GENERATED, not the author's statement.”

`document_md.contains_generated_text` is always false; generated meanings and image descriptions remain separate derivatives. A missing asset records `missing_asset` without a fabricated path or digest. The public renderer refuses non-`author_open` packages.

## 8. Quality observations and readiness

Extraction and semantic observations remain separate; there is no combined score. Extraction statuses are `pass`, `partial`, `adapter_required`, `failed`, `not_measured`. Check results use the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md): `pass`, `fail`, `unknown`, `not_applicable`. Each check has a unique identifier and method.

**BPK-R032.** A `not_applicable` check MUST state the inapplicability reason in `detail`.

**BPK-R033.** Extraction `pass` MUST contain at least one applicable passing check, no failed or unknown check, and matching seen/expected unit counts when those counts are supplied.

An empty checklist or an all-inapplicable checklist cannot prove successful extraction. `adapter_required` contradicts available converter output; `pass` or `partial` contradicts absent converter output.

CER is either measured with a value, reference class and sample size, or `not_measured` with null. A null is not a measured zero. Semantic status is `not_built`, `not_measured`, `built_unreviewed` or `reviewed`; the first two require empty object and relation files.

**BPK-R034.** Every semantic review MUST identify `review_id`, `reviewer_id`, `producer_id`, reviewer type, independence basis, time, result, the exact object and relation identifiers examined, and the current object-file, relation-file and readable-file digests.

**BPK-R035.** `sample_size` counts distinct reviewed objects, not relations or checklist items, and MUST equal the object-ID list length.

Reviews cannot name nonexistent records. A review of earlier bytes cannot be transferred to new bytes by changing only the timestamp.

**BPK-R036.** The `knowledge_ready` profile uses **complete coverage**, not a statistical sample: every object and relation MUST be included, every included record MUST be `reviewed`, the result MUST be `supported`, and the declared reviewer MUST be a human or blind agent distinct from the producer.

Same-session review, partial support, unsupported results and omitted records cannot earn this status.

**BPK-R052.** There MUST be at least one object.

A narrower review is retained honestly under `partial_pilot`.

These checks verify declared scope, separation and byte bindings. They do not prove that a human review occurred or that identities are independent. The synthetic fixture uses explicitly invented reviewer identities for testing. Authenticating observations belongs to the consuming trust boundary.

### Total package-status decision table

**BPK-R037.** The validator MUST compute the first matching row below and require equality with the submitted `package_status`.

All well-formed combinations receive one result; contradictory component/check declarations are additionally rejected rather than assigned a success status.

| Priority | Condition | Derived status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Source integrity is `mismatch`, even if completeness is bibliographic | `blocked_integrity` |
| 2 | Completeness is `bibliography_only` | `bibliography_only` |
| 3 | Converter output is absent | `adapter_required` |
| 4 | Semantic layer is `not_built` or `not_measured`, with zero objects and relations | `structure_only` |
| 5 | Full, verified source; extraction passes; complete independent declared review supports all reviewed records | `knowledge_ready` |
| 6 | Any remaining well-formed converted package | `partial_pilot` |

**BPK-R038.** `blocked_integrity`, `bibliography_only` and `adapter_required` MUST omit both document and readable components.

**BPK-R039.** `structure_only`, `partial_pilot` and `knowledge_ready` MUST contain both.

A reviewed package with unverified integrity or partial source coverage is therefore representable as `partial_pilot`. Extraction failure remains visible even when files are structurally present.

## 9. Correction impact and reassessment

**BPK-R040.** An accepted correction MUST invalidate every object explicitly named in `depends_on_objects` **or** whose source anchor overlaps the edit.

The producer's dependency list cannot suppress the overlap rule. Section/block and slide/block matches are exact; intervals and comparable page boxes use intersection; JSON pointers use equality or ancestor relationships. Sheet ranges conservatively invalidate the whole same-sheet scope; incomparable geometry is treated as potentially overlapping.

**BPK-R041.** An affected object MUST be stale or rejected until its `reviewed_edit_ids` explicitly names the accepted corrections and a supported, independent declared review covers that object and the current readable and semantic file digests.

References to unknown or unaccepted edit identifiers are rejected. A stale proposal cannot be cleared by this accepted-edit mechanism. Dependent relations follow section 5. This is a declared reassessment record, not evidence that a producer's semantic reasoning was correct.

## 10. Rights and publication layers

`rights.license_class` is `author_open`, `third_party_restricted` or `unknown`.

**BPK-R042.** Only `author_open` MAY declare the package publication `license` (`CC-BY-4.0` or `Apache-2.0`) and nonempty `publishable_layers`.

**BPK-R043.** Restricted or unknown works MUST omit that licence field and leave the publication layer list empty.

This blocks book text carried through semantic chunks as well as through document files. No quotation budget or right to republish is inferred from extraction, review or a producer-supplied licence string.

Private processing of an otherwise valid restricted package is outside public projection.

**BPK-R044.** The projection function MUST return no rows for such a package.

This conservative producer rule implements the established exclusion of third-party book text from the repository; it is not a legal finding about possible uses elsewhere. Any separately authored derivative requires its own source/rights assessment and publication decision.

## 11. Candidate export and admission runtime

**BPK-R045.** `candidate_projection(package_directory)` MUST validate a private immutable byte snapshot and compute the rendering from that snapshot itself.

It does not accept a raw manifest plus caller-selected Markdown.

**BPK-R046.** It MUST return no rows on validation failure or for `blocked_integrity`, `bibliography_only` or `adapter_required`, or without an allowed open publication licence.

| Package element | Candidate projection |
| --- | --- |
| Allowed document/readable Markdown | One proposed row for its exact bytes |
| Allowed knowledge layer | One proposed `knowledge.md` row for the full protected rendering |
| JSON records, assets and quality file | Retained in the package; not separate Markdown export rows |
| Unlisted layer or prohibited status/rights class | No row |
| Runtime admission | Remains `not_measured` |

Rows identify `articles/book-packages/<package_id>/<file>`, digest, holder, licence, limits and `status: proposed`. These paths fit the exporter path syntax but are not automatically allowlisted. The existing `KnowledgeContractCandidateExport/v2` exporter still requires its own admitted scope, exact merged revision and reviewed publication decision.

**BPK-R047.** The producer MUST NOT widen that scope or treat a candidate row as publication or runtime admission.

The [API integration appendix](../../../appendices/api-integration/APPENDIX.md) distinguishes repository publication, index admission and actual service results. No live consumer acceptance of this new profile is claimed by local conformance tests.

## 12. Validation and evidence limits

The JSON Schema checks record shape, fixed vocabularies and conditional fields. The package validator adds digest and count checks; identity and endpoint consistency; anchor mapping and geometry; byte-exact reversible replay; correction impact; extraction conjunction; review scope and file bindings; complete status derivation; and publication rights boundaries.

**BPK-R048.** The conformance suite MUST retain each regression's expected cause, including negative review, unknown extraction, restricted licence bypass, arbitrary rendering, Markdown structure injection, stale correction, rejected endpoint and obsolete profile.

Its status-table test enumerates 3,240 input combinations; enumeration tests the decision table, not 3,240 historical book experiments. Structural incoherence is tested separately.

The package does not establish source fidelity, truth, real reviewer independence, unseen source geometry, authentic licence ownership or downstream admission. Generated descriptions are separate from the media they describe. The linked [transcription study](../../studies/source-transcription-and-uncertainty/STUDY.md) reports historical evidence; it is not validation of the new wire profile.

## 13. Compatibility and migration

Version 1 is preserved at immutable [source revision ed2e188c](https://github.com/Arcanada-one/knowledge-contract/tree/ed2e188c89a1a50a187f3d55bf32baab54c87f7e). Version 2 is intentionally stricter and adds observations, scoped review, the readable package identifier, explicit validation profile and semantic vocabularies.

**BPK-R049.** Existing record tags MUST NOT simply be rewritten: missing observations remain missing, unsupported edits remain unsupported, and review of old bytes does not establish review of migrated bytes.

**BPK-R050.** A migration MUST create a new package release, retain the original revision, identify every mapping or dropped unsupported field, revalidate digests and edits, and obtain any newly required review.

**BPK-R051.** It MUST NOT invent integrity observations, reviewer identities or author attribution.

V2 builders emit empty semantic layers until interpretation is actually performed. The publication edition, package profile, validator build and admission-runtime version remain distinct identities.

Credit and licence: Knowledge Contract Arcana, <https://contract.place> (CC BY 4.0). See [attribution](../../../ATTRIBUTION.md).

BLOB engineering/specifications/cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md 742982eabd0d797ccc5bba4c8472d25a4236c84baed21281f13a7601a4ce2f39 original 12247

artifact: "engineering-specification"

# Cache Identity, Dependency Tracking, and Invalidation

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

This specification governs how a saved result is identified, found as a candidate, invalidated after change and admitted at a serve boundary. Equal bytes, equal recipes, semantic suitability and current authorization are separate relations.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). The architecture is supported by conditional arguments and bounded models, not by a claim of a deployed distributed coherence protocol.

**CCH-R001.** Every implementation MUST state its consistency assumptions and failure behavior.

See [controlled reuse](../../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md).

## 2. Identity layers

| Identity | What it fixes | What it does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Content identity | Exact bytes or canonical structured value in a named hash domain | Meaning, authorship, truth or permission |
| Build identity | Declared inputs, recipe, parameters and relevant environment | Actual execution or deterministic output |
| Lookup identity | A candidate class for a query, recipient and policy | Eligibility of every retrieved candidate |
| Current head | The selected revision within a namespace and generation | Timeless suitability for all consumers |

**CCH-R002.** A lookup MUST be followed by request/artifact descriptor comparison and the required admission checks.

Negation, quantifiers, recipient, purpose and scope are not dispensable variations of a similar query.

**CCH-R003.** A digest collision assumption and the selected byte or canonical-value domain MUST be stated when digest equality is used as identity evidence.

The implemented byte domains are registered in the [status exchange profile](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile); an unnamed canonicalization is unsupported.

A request descriptor fixes the fields below. The artifact descriptor records the corresponding values for which the saved artifact was prepared and assessed.

| Descriptor component | Comparison rule |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose and recipient identifiers | Exact identities; aliases need an independently established identity mapping |
| Requirements/criterion references | Exact immutable revisions and digests, or a separately checked entailment of every new mandatory requirement |
| Representation and required profile references | Exact profile/version support or an explicit checked adapter with loss account |
| Subject, scope, quantifier and protected operators | Exact structure under the named representation profile, or an explicit scoped preservation argument; similarity is insufficient |
| Constraint and authority references | Complete current mandatory set, with no ignored constraint-bearing profile; current grants are rechecked |
| Required output/delivery form and budget | Actual delivered form satisfies the requested profile and actual resource bounds |
| Temporal mode and dependency snapshot | Requested historical/current/bounded-stale policy plus complete current read-set checks |

**CCH-R004.** The descriptor comparison MUST return an itemized `pass`, `fail`, `unknown` or justified `not_applicable` for the relevant components.

Missing fields, unknown profile semantics or an unproved implication remain unknown.

**CCH-R005.** The algorithm MUST NOT equate two requests merely because their lookup key, text embedding or source bytes agree.

Descriptor equality is a preliminary gate, not a grant of current authority. This architecture does not serialize full descriptors; a complete descriptor wire mapping remains a profile-specific obligation.

## 3. Dependency tracking

**CCH-R006.** The read set MUST contain every declared dependency material to the claimed reuse guarantee, including source revisions, required selectors, profiles, rules, request requirements, compiler behavior and authorization state.

Dependency kind determines the impact rule. Relocation of identical bytes can preserve content identity while changing accessibility; a changed interpretation can invalidate applicability without changing a source file.

**CCH-R007.** Negative results and exhaustive claims MUST also track their universe or index generation and completeness assumptions.

New content can invalidate “no match” or “all matches” without modifying any previously selected object. Top-k retrieval does not supply that completeness premise.

**CCH-R008.** Unknown impact MUST produce re-evaluation or a conservative unresolved result under the policy.

**CCH-R009.** It MUST NOT be inferred harmless simply because the old key still matches.

## 4. Change events and ordering

A change event identifies the affected dependency, previous and new revisions or epochs, event identity, ordering namespace and impact class.

**CCH-R010.** Epochs are nonnegative integers and a relevant change MUST strictly advance the affected dependency epoch even when its content bytes are unchanged.

An authority revocation changes admissibility without necessarily changing content.

The implemented `KCDependencyChange/v1` record in the [exchange schema](../../../schemas/contract-observation.schema.json) supplies `event_id`, `namespace`, `sequence`, `dependency_id`, `old_epoch`, `new_epoch`, old/new revision references, kind, impact, observer and time.

**CCH-R011.** `new_epoch` MUST exceed `old_epoch`; `revoked` events MUST carry `impact: blocked`.

Null revision references denote absence of an addressable revision, not established content equality. Event kinds are `revision_changed`, `revoked`, `availability_changed` and `unknown_change`. Impact uses the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies).

**CCH-R012.** A receiver MUST deduplicate an identical event identifier only when the complete event bytes agree, detect conflicting reuse of that identifier, and treat a missing or reordered sequence in its declared ordering namespace as a reconciliation requirement.

The profile serializes these facts; it does not provide event transport or certify complete event delivery. No inference of harmlessness follows from a missed event.

**CCH-R013.** If event loss is possible, reconciliation MUST compare observed dependency state with authoritative state before renewing affected admission claims.

**CCH-R014.** A disconnected system MAY serve under an explicitly bounded policy, but MUST NOT claim immediate global revocation when its consistency protocol cannot establish it.

## 5. Guarded publication and serving

A **head namespace** identifies one ordered stream of current-head decisions. Its **generation** is a nonnegative counter that strictly increases on every head change, including rollback, and every revocation applying to that head.

**CCH-R015.** Generation values MUST NOT be reused after rollback, restart, restore or counter exhaustion.

**CCH-R016.** A runtime unable to recover the counter safely MUST establish a fresh namespace and invalidate old read sets.

The exchange subset bounds integers at 2^53−1; exhaustion fails closed rather than wrapping.

**CCH-R017.** A worker's read set MUST contain the namespace and generation, every material dependency's exact revision and epoch, and relevant authority/policy epochs.

Completeness of that dependency set is an explicit premise of the guarantee, not a fact inferred from a cache key.

**CCH-R018.** Publication MUST make one linearizable decision over the following predicate: the current namespace and head generation equal the worker's expected values; every declared material dependency still has the observed epoch/revision; current authority and policy permit this exact output/use; and all required build checks remain satisfied.

**CCH-R019.** The head update and increment of generation MUST occur atomically with that decision.

A separate dependency check followed by a compare-and-set on the head alone is insufficient: revocation can occur between them.

**CCH-R020.** Every relevant revocation MUST advance its authority/dependency epoch and the affected head generation within the declared decision boundary.

**CCH-R021.** A late worker MUST NOT restore an older head, undo a revocation or publish against an unchanged-looking dependency after an intervening change.

**CCH-R022.** A deployment lacking an atomic boundary over these conditions MUST fail closed or explicitly offer a weaker named consistency profile; it cannot claim this strict guarantee.

**CCH-R023.** The serve guard MUST validate the exact output, relevant current dependency epochs and current authority at the actual serve consistency point.

Build-time validation does not authorize a later unguarded serve. Locks, transactions or a justified validation-and-commit protocol are mechanisms only when their scope covers the whole predicate. Remote effects outside that boundary retain their own reconciliation semantics.

**CCH-R024.** A lease MUST define scope, expiry, revocation behavior, clock assumptions and partition behavior.

A strict revocation claim cannot treat an unobservable revocation as harmless unless its explicitly adopted authorization semantics permit the stated delay.

The [reference model and regression tests](../../../tools/test_contract_profile.py) serialize the namespace, generation, dependency-epoch and revocation part of this predicate under one in-memory lock. They do not evaluate output-specific grants, semantic validity or build checks. They exhibit a late publisher, a changed dependency under a current head token, revocation between read and publish, concurrent publishers, exhaustion of either a head or dependency counter, and rollback without generation reuse. They are finite local checks, not evidence of distributed deployment or durable crash recovery.

## 6. Storage, recovery and observability

Residency and eligibility are independent. Eviction can remove a valid copy; retention can preserve an invalid one.

**CCH-R025.** Recovery MUST verify content identity and rebuild the current eligibility decision rather than restoring a historical positive verdict as current fact.

**CCH-R026.** Logs SHOULD record lookup candidate, compared descriptors, dependency observations, admission decision, publication generation and actual serve result.

**CCH-R027.** Logging MUST respect the recipient's disclosure rights.

Required audit retention is a separate policy from cache residence time.

## 7. Acceptance cases

Required tests include: identical query text with a changed recipient; changed source under a matching lookup key; a new object invalidating a negative result; a lost invalidation event found by reconciliation; a late build attempting to overwrite a newer head; expiry exactly at a hard deadline; and revocation between preliminary check and serve.

**CCH-R028.** Each test MUST identify the simulated or real consistency boundary.

The [reuse study](../../studies/reuse-admission-and-cost-controls/STUDY.md) reports the available evidence and its deployment limits. [RFC 9111](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#http-caching) is a reference for HTTP cache semantics, not proof that the semantic or authority checks defined here have been implemented. Existing bounded profiles and retained results are identified in the [compatibility appendix](../../../appendices/source-map/APPENDIX.md#4-protocol-compatibility).

BLOB engineering/specifications/compiled-artifact-contract/SPECIFICATION.md 2cc5e781565136b5ad9fdbb2facae47d58564b1e300173d0a91a2c6cf16d5a82 original 7683

name: "compiled-artifact-contract"
title: "Compiled Knowledge Artifacts and Reproducible Derivation"
artifact: "engineering-specification"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"

description: "Saved derivatives with exact inputs, recipes, dependency manifests, templates and output evidence."
