# Compiled Knowledge Artifacts and Reproducible Derivation

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

A compiled knowledge artifact is a saved derivative produced by a declared extraction, selection, transformation or composition. It connects a result with its inputs, recipe, dependencies, requirements, output representation and evidence. Its availability can avoid repeated work, but availability alone does not justify reuse.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

**CKA-R001.** A conformance claim MUST identify the artifact kind, representation profile and checks performed. [Controlled knowledge reuse](../../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md) provides the model; [cache identity](../cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md) and [reuse policy](../reuse-policy/SPECIFICATION.md) govern later applications.

## 2. Result kinds and minimum information

| Result kind | Additional minimum obligation |
| --- | --- |
| Retrieval result | Query semantics, searched snapshot, procedure limits and candidate identities |
| Selected context | Requirement coverage, mandatory closure, projection and resource account |
| Derived explanation or answer | Supported claims, intended audience, losses and required uncertainty |
| Dependency closure | Roots, relation policy, exact graph snapshot and unresolved edges |
| Parameterized template | Allowed substitutions, domain restrictions and instance validation rules |
| Scoped negative result | The exact negative claim, finite scope, completeness premise and invalidators |

These kinds do not exhaust all possible derivatives.

**CKA-R002.** An extension MUST state its additional obligations and compatibility rules.

## 3. Artifact and build records

**CKA-R003.** The artifact MUST identify immutable output bytes or a precisely canonicalized structured value, its hash domain, representation profile, content identity, source and rule-set revisions, recipe identity, and the producing attempt.

A content digest establishes equality within its encoding assumptions; it does not authenticate the author or establish source truth.

**CKA-R004.** The build recipe MUST include exact input references, transformation definition versions, relevant parameters, and environment dependencies that can change the claimed result.

A build identity records this declaration. Reproducibility additionally requires actual successful reruns under a stated equivalence criterion. A matching recipe hash does not prove that the recipe was executed or that nondeterministic outputs are equal.

**CKA-R005.** An attempt record MUST distinguish the `planned`, `ready`, `running`, `paused` and `finished` execution phases from the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies); outcome; stop reason; output references if any; and observed effects.

**CKA-R006.** A failed attempt MUST NOT create a fictional successful artifact.

Partial outputs can be retained as partial with their limitations.

## 4. Dependency manifest

**CKA-R007.** The manifest MUST include each declared dependency's identity, revision, role, requirement status, relevant validity conditions and change sensitivity.

Dependencies can include sources, selectors, profiles, rules, compiler versions, authority epochs, queries and requirements. Omitting a relevant dependency invalidates any claim that the manifest is complete for that use.

**CKA-R008.** A result based on absence or exhaustive enumeration MUST bind the universe or index snapshot and completeness procedure.

Adding a new matching item can invalidate a negative result even if none of its previously read positive inputs changed.

**CKA-R009.** The manifest MUST distinguish a navigational citation from an operational dependency.

**CKA-R010.** It SHOULD explain why each operational dependency is material, so a reviewer can identify both missing dependencies and unjustified over-invalidation.

## 5. Templates and instances

A template defines allowed substitutions, protected structure and the conditions under which an instance may be produced. Every instance receives its own identity, bound parameters, requirements and admission decision. The template's age or prior acceptance does not automatically transfer to an instance.

**CKA-R011.** Parameter substitution MUST NOT remove scope, change a protected quantifier or carry authority from one recipient to another without an explicit rule.

Unvalidated parameters yield an unresolved or rejected instance. If instantiation changes the meaning outside the declared template domain, the result is a new derivation rather than a valid instance.

## 6. Evidence and publication boundary

**CKA-R012.** Evidence MUST bind to the exact artifact and check.

Structural validity, reconstruction equality, task sufficiency, current applicability and execution success remain separate observations. An artifact can be immutable and byte-correct while ineligible for a new request.

**CKA-R013.** Publishing a mutable current pointer MUST follow the complete atomic predicate in [cache identity §5](../cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md#5-guarded-publication-and-serving), including dependency and authority epochs in the same decision as the head update.

**CKA-R014.** A build that finished successfully under an old generation MUST NOT overwrite a newer or revoked head.

**CKA-R015.** The final admission decision MUST be valid at the actual serve boundary, not only when the build began.

## 7. Acceptance cases and limits

**CKA-R016.** A conformance suite MUST cover an altered input with an unchanged lookup key, a recipe change, an unsuccessful build, a template parameter outside its domain, a stale publication generation, a negative result invalidated by corpus growth, and evidence for the wrong output.

**CKA-R017.** The expected result MUST be an explicit new build, refusal, unresolved state or valid guarded reuse under a named policy.

The [reuse-admission study](../../studies/reuse-admission-and-cost-controls/STUDY.md) separates analytical policies, bounded checks and measured outcomes. The [projection study](../../studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md) reports finite transformations and revision behavior. Neither establishes a deployed distributed cache or a general semantic equivalence solver.

## 8. Compatibility and migration

**CKA-R018.** A migration MUST retain the old artifact and hash domain, identify the mapping and its losses, and create a new representation record.

A new document edition does not change an existing binary or wire protocol. Earlier bounded profiles are named in the [compatibility appendix](../../../appendices/source-map/APPENDIX.md#4-protocol-compatibility); implementations claiming additional behavior require corresponding evidence.

BLOB engineering/specifications/context-package/SPECIFICATION.md 5bba82777b44dc0cf6b1b0c218292c1476c0271806acef195db6c3b8a9448e41 original 7834

name: "context-package"
title: "Context Packages: Coverage, Dependencies, and Delivery"
artifact: "engineering-specification"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"

description: "Requirements for selected context, coverage, mandatory dependencies, budgets and observed delivery."

# Context Packages: Coverage, Dependencies, and Delivery

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

A context package is a versioned selection of knowledge, conditions and grounds prepared for one declared use under a resource budget. It allows inspection of what was selected, why it is sufficient or incomplete, which dependencies were included, and what the recipient actually receives.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). The package supports an answer, plan or execution stage. It is not itself a grant to act or evidence that the intended result occurred. See [retrieval and context composition](../../../papers/retrieval-and-context-composition/PAPER.md) for the formal distinction between coverage and search completeness.

## 2. Required package description

**CTX-R001.** A conforming package MUST contain or reference its identity and revision; task and requirements revision; purpose and recipient; source and corpus snapshots; selected unit revisions; mandatory dependency closure; slot-level coverage; declared search scope and assurance; applicable constraints; representation and delivery requirements; resource account; and the observations supporting its status.

**CTX-R002.** Each requirement slot MUST identify the subject, quantifier, required conditions and supporting units.

**CTX-R003.** Joint requirements MUST be supported for the same subject and revision.

Evidence for a property of one object and a different property of another cannot satisfy a conjunction about one object.

**CTX-R004.** The package MUST identify omitted requirements and unresolved dependencies.

If a necessary ground is restricted, an authorized summary or substitute requires its own justification. Silently removing the ground while retaining a claim of complete support is prohibited.

## 3. Selection and closure

Selection starts from candidate units and computes the closure of the mandatory relations active for this use. Navigation links do not automatically join the closure. Unknown or missing required endpoints prevent a complete-package claim.

**CTX-R005.** The finite set-theoretic closure exists even with cycles, but a particular executable profile MAY require an acyclic dependency graph.

**CTX-R006.** That restriction MUST be stated and checked.

Rejection of a cycle by such a profile is not a claim that the abstract closure is undefined.

Adding units can introduce new dependencies and costs.

**CTX-R007.** A composer MUST recalculate closure and budget after a change.

**CTX-R008.** It MUST NOT infer that a larger text is always safer or more useful: additional content may conflict, distract, disclose protected information or exceed the recipient's usable capacity.

## 4. Coverage and assurance

Coverage is assessed separately for candidate set, assembled package and final output.

**CTX-R009.** Each report MUST bind to its own stage and subject revision.

A supported candidate set does not make a truncated package supported; a supported package does not prove that an answer used it correctly.

| Coverage value | Meaning within the declared requirements and scope |
| --- | --- |
| supported | Every mandatory slot has the required established support |
| partial | A supported part exists, with named missing or unresolved obligations |
| unsupported | The evaluated scope provides no required support under the chosen procedure |
| conflicted | Material incompatible grounds prevent a single admitted conclusion |
| unknown | The available observations cannot establish the coverage state |

Search assurance is a separate dimension: `candidate_only`, `exhaustive_in_scope`, or `incomplete_procedure`, using the [canonical token definitions](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies). A request for two examples may be supported without exhaustive search. A request for all matching objects requires an additional completeness obligation.

**CTX-R010.** An empty top-k result MUST NOT be reported as proof of global absence.

## 5. Budget and projection

**CTX-R011.** The budget MUST account for the actual delivery context: system and task instructions, relevant history, selected material, tool descriptions, anticipated tool results, output allowance and margin where applicable.

A character count is not an exact token measurement.

**CTX-R012.** Estimated and measured quantities MUST be distinguishable and identify the tokenizer or measurement method.

**CTX-R013.** A projection MUST retain protected units, their relations and task-relevant uncertainty.

**CTX-R014.** It MUST identify excluded units and losses.

An output that reconstructs the selected bytes exactly can still be semantically incomplete if selection omitted an exception. Byte equality and task sufficiency therefore require different checks.

**CTX-R015.** When the mandatory closure cannot fit, the composer MUST return a partial result, negotiate an allowed narrower scope, change representation with justified preservation, or refuse.

**CTX-R016.** It MUST NOT silently discard a mandatory condition to meet the limit.

## 6. Delivery and receipt

The manifest describes what should be delivered; a receipt records what was observed at the recipient boundary. A media URL does not establish byte availability, display, legibility or understanding.

**CTX-R017.** The delivery check MUST match the task's representation requirements and the declared reader capabilities.

**CTX-R018.** Any output assessment MUST name the exact delivered package and requirements revision.

An acknowledgement of receipt or comprehension is not a capability assessment, agreement to execute, or authorization.

## 7. Acceptance and evidence

**CTX-R019.** Acceptance cases MUST include an unresolved dependency, cross-object conjunction, missing quantifier coverage, a budget overflow caused by closure, omitted mandatory media, an outdated requirement revision, and a final output that drops a supported condition.

**CTX-R020.** For each, the check MUST distinguish package construction from downstream use.

The [dependency-closure and delivery study](../../studies/dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md) includes an executable finite replay of all 4,096 subsets of the 12 skill roots and 24,576 single-element inclusion relations on a 110-node dependency graph. This verifies the specified graph behavior, not the completeness of dependencies extracted from arbitrary sources. The [projection study](../../studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md) examines downstream representations and reports the limits of measured minimality.

## 8. Compatibility

Legacy coverage fields can be imported only with their subject and semantics. Missing source snapshots, budget measurements or delivery observations remain unknown. A new document edition does not retrospectively upgrade earlier illustrative examples into executed packages. Terms are defined in the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

BLOB engineering/specifications/contract-family/SPECIFICATION.md 310cd3f151275739a2391d2c8d8b7c23ad64b46f4c1a56717f7f0975866f60c8 original 8226
---
name: "contract-family"
title: "Contracts for Knowledge Operations"
artifact: "engineering-specification"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"
license: "CC-BY-4.0"
description: "Common preconditions, invariants, postconditions, effects and evidence for knowledge operations."
---

# Contracts for Knowledge Operations

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

An operation contract states when an operation may run, what it preserves or changes, which outcomes are permitted, and what evidence supports the reported outcome. Its promise is local to a declared input, requirements revision and environment. Completing one operation does not necessarily satisfy the entire Work Item.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

**KOP-R001.** This document specifies operation semantics; a conforming adapter MUST also identify its representation profile and executable checks.

The [transformation paper](../../../papers/constrained-transformations/PAPER.md) supplies the conditional formal model.

## 2. Common contract record

**KOP-R002.** Every operation definition MUST identify the following:

| Field group | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Definition | Operation name, immutable definition version and supported input/output profiles |
| Purpose | The local goal and its relationship to a Work Item and requirements revision |
| Inputs | Exact artifact revisions, cardinalities, relevant environment and missing-input behavior |
| Preconditions | Conditions that authorize and enable the attempt; checking methods and trust boundaries |
| Invariants | Properties preserved throughout the operation's declared observable scope |
| Postconditions | Conditions attached to each allowed outcome, including partial and refused outcomes |
| Effects | Read/write scope, external effects, repeat policy, reconciliation and compensation behavior |
| Resources | Budget, deadline, maximum attempts and stop rules |
| Evidence | Subject bindings, observation method and the limits of the evaluator |

**KOP-R003.** Preconditions, invariants and postconditions MUST remain separate.

A later successful observation does not establish that an invariant held at every earlier step. Evidence of an output's shape does not establish its semantic correctness.

## 3. Operation families

The following functional families organize implementations. They are responsibilities, not a requirement to deploy separate services.

| Family | Preservation obligation | Principal failure mode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Acquire and address | Source identity, version, locator and acquisition provenance | An unavailable or substituted source is treated as observed |
| Interpret and extract | Attribution, negation, quantifiers, exceptions and unresolved readings | A source claim becomes an unqualified fact |
| Search and compare | Query semantics, scope, candidate provenance and comparison criterion | Similarity becomes equivalence or completeness |
| Select and compose | Mandatory dependency closure, joint identity, coverage and total budget | Necessary conditions disappear during shortening |
| Transform and render | Declared protected observations, representation requirements and losses | A readable description is substituted for essential media |
| Deliver and acknowledge | Recipient, representation, revision and delivery observation | A locator is mistaken for delivered content |
| Plan and execute | Work bindings, capability, authority, attempt identity and effects | A plan or timeout is mistaken for a known execution outcome |
| Assess and revise | Exact subject, method, new evidence and preserved history | A verdict is transferred to another revision |
| Store and reuse | Dependency pins, current applicability, authorization and invalidation | A cache hit bypasses the admission decision |

**KOP-R004.** An implementation MAY expose narrower operations within these families.

**KOP-R005.** Every exposed operation MUST state which of these responsibilities it owns and which it delegates.

**KOP-R006.** It MUST NOT hide a necessary responsibility between adapters.

## 4. Composition

**KOP-R007.** For operations A then B, every allowed successful output of A used by B MUST satisfy B's preconditions.

**KOP-R008.** A shared invariant MUST survive both transitions.

Failure to establish that relation leaves composition unresolved even when each operation passes its isolated tests.

**KOP-R009.** A semantic transformation MUST declare its protected observations: for example the truth conditions of a proposition, the order of procedural steps, or the labels and connections of a diagram.

Equality of those observations is a narrower claim than equality of all meanings.

**KOP-R010.** Lossy transformations MUST retain a loss account.

An inverse can be claimed only within a declared domain for which reconstruction has been justified.

Operations with effects require a separate composition policy. A local transaction around a record does not make an external service atomic.

**KOP-R011.** A timeout after dispatch MUST preserve an unknown-effect state until a domain-specific reconciliation establishes what occurred.

## 5. Outcomes and evidence

The outcome vocabulary is `success`, `partial`, `refused`, `failed` and `unresolved`, as defined in the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies). `ready` is a lifecycle or run phase, not an operation outcome. Stop reasons explain why control ended; effect observations describe what is known about external changes.

**KOP-R012.** These dimensions MUST NOT be collapsed into a single completion flag.

A comparison operation can succeed by reporting non-equivalence. A refusal can correctly enforce a constraint without satisfying the user's original goal.

**KOP-R013.** Evidence MUST identify the definition version, input and output revisions, attempt, relevant requirements, checker and check result.

**KOP-R014.** An inherited observation MUST name the premise that permits inheritance.

**KOP-R015.** Checks of supplied claims and checks of independently observed conditions MUST be distinguishable.

## 6. Acceptance cases

**KOP-R016.** A conforming adapter MUST exercise valid input; invalid input; unknown mandatory preconditions; a protected-property violation; partial output; an exhausted budget; a stale requirement revision; and, for effectful operations, a timeout with uncertain effect.

**KOP-R017.** A composition test MUST include a valid A output that violates B's precondition.

The expected result is refusal or unresolved composition, not automatic continuation.

The [dependency and delivery study](../../studies/dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md) supplies a finite closure replay. The [executable projection study](../../studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md) supplies bounded projection and revision evidence. Their finite results do not certify every family in this specification.

## 7. Representation and evolution

The architecture does not require a new global artifact type for each operation. A versioned definition can be represented as an addressed record linked from attempts and evidence.

**KOP-R018.** Legacy example envelopes remain interpretable under their original profiles; an adapter MUST disclose any loss when mapping them to this family.

An operation definition change creates a new version. Attempts keep the definition they used. Reassessment under the new definition is a new observation, not a retrospective claim that the earlier attempt followed it. See [knowledge evolution](../knowledge-evolution/SPECIFICATION.md) and the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md).

BLOB engineering/specifications/knowledge-contract-core/SPECIFICATION.md 81cb01a8326eaeb0c14e929609c52cc85e5a344c61f3744d2e0def228cdc60d5 original 9729
---
name: "knowledge-contract-core"
title: "Knowledge Contract: Core Model and Admission Requirements"
artifact: "engineering-specification"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"
license: "CC-BY-4.0"
description: "The nine core groups, first-class constraints, profiles, admission and execution requirements."

# Knowledge Contract: Core Model and Admission Requirements

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

A Knowledge Contract binds a body of knowledge to its intended use, grounds, dependencies, constraints, and assessment. It lets a recipient inspect what is offered, which conditions matter, and what has actually been checked. It does not turn a source assertion into a fact or an instruction into permission to act.

This specification defines the architecture of a conforming implementation. Capitalized requirement words follow [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

**KCC-R001.** Conformance MUST identify the implemented profile, its version, the reader, and the assessed scope.

A claim of full conformance cannot rest on a schema check alone. Publication edition 1.1 is the edition of this document; serialized profiles retain their own versions. The [compatibility record](../../../appendices/source-map/APPENDIX.md#4-protocol-compatibility) identifies the existing bounded implementation without renaming it.

## 2. Core model

The contract has nine logical groups.

**KCC-R002.** A serializer MAY organize them differently if its mapping preserves their obligations.

| Group | Required meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Identity | Stable object identity, immutable revision, contract definition and representation profile versions |
| Purpose | Intended use, task and requirements revision, recipient, required profiles |
| Content | Addressable units, their semantic roles, source references and protected conditions |
| Scope | Conditions of applicability and explicit unknown, inapplicable or withheld values |
| Grounds | The source revision, locator, derivation and evidence supporting each relevant claim |
| Dependencies | Required relationships distinguished from optional reading links |
| Obligations | Preconditions, invariants, preservation duties, coverage requirements and acceptance obligations |
| Projection | Delivered representation, included and omitted units, declared losses and transformations |
| Observations | Assessments bound to a subject revision, method, observer, time and limitations |

**KCC-R003.** An empty list MUST mean an established empty set within a declared scope.

**KCC-R004.** It MUST NOT stand for an unknown set.

**KCC-R005.** A profile MAY represent a genuinely irrelevant group as not applicable with a reason.

**KCC-R006.** It MUST NOT silently omit a required obligation because no evidence is available.

The contract's immutable content and the current decision to use it are distinct records. Revoking a grant changes admissibility for a use; it does not rewrite what the earlier contract said.

## 3. Constraints are first-class obligations

A **constraint** restricts an admissible interpretation, transformation, delivery or action.

**KCC-R007.** Each constraint MUST have an identity, subject, scope, predicate or human-readable rule, source of authority when relevant, severity, and a checking method or an explicit unresolved status.

**KCC-R008.** An implementation MUST retain its applicability conditions and dependencies.

The [artifact registry](../artifact-registry/SPECIFICATION.md#4-constraints-and-professional-artifacts) defines the readable `CONSTRAINT.md` artifact.

Four distinctions are mandatory:

| Kind | Question | Example of an abstract rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Applicability condition | Under what circumstances does the knowledge apply? | The input belongs to the stated domain |
| Action constraint | What conduct is forbidden or bounded? | The operation may write only the named resource |
| Capability requirement | Can this executor perform the required method? | An assessment covers this skill and environment |
| Acceptance criterion | What demonstrates the requested result? | The delivered output satisfies the pinned criterion |

**KCC-R009.** A constraint can also be an acceptance obligation, but the relationship MUST be explicit.

Success at an output criterion does not excuse a prohibited action used to obtain it. A skill description does not establish capability. A role assignment does not by itself supply a grant.

**KCC-R010.** When profiles compose, all applicable mandatory constraints MUST remain in force unless an identified authority, authenticated at the same declared trust boundary as evidence, has explicitly resolved a conflict within its verified authority.

**KCC-R011.** Load order, a later paragraph, or a more convenient result MUST NOT implicitly override a restriction.

A contradiction blocks the affected use or produces a narrowed, explicitly permitted alternative.

## 4. Profiles and compatibility

The supported profile families are professional work, dialogue, documents, reasoning, visual representations, and reuse. Each profile declares its version, why it is present, whether it is required, and its payload. The need for a profile follows from the declared purpose and requirements; a parser is not assumed to infer it correctly from arbitrary natural language.

**KCC-R012.** A reader MUST advertise supported representations and required extensions.

An unknown required profile makes the contract unusable for that request. A profile carrying any mandatory constraint is required regardless of a producer-supplied optional flag.

**KCC-R013.** A reader MUST derive requiredness from the trusted request/policy and known constraint references as well as profile declarations, and MUST block an unknown constraint-bearing profile.

An opaque profile whose constraint relevance cannot be established is unknown for admission, not harmless.

**KCC-R014.** Only an extension established as irrelevant to every mandatory obligation MAY be retained as optional opaque data, with the limitation reported.

Preserving its bytes does not verify its semantics. Compatibility is directional: a new reader accepting an old record does not prove that an old reader can safely rewrite the new record.

## 5. Admission and execution

**KCC-R015.** Admission MUST evaluate the exact contract revision, requirements, recipient, environment, applicable constraints, mandatory dependencies, and current authority.

Each mandatory check yields pass, fail, unknown, or justified not applicable.

**KCC-R016.** Unknown MUST NOT be promoted to pass.

An execution additionally binds the Work Item revision, requirements revision, contract revision or digest, attempt, executor, relevant grants and capabilities, output identity, and criterion-specific evidence. Receipt of a plan is not execution. A successful process exit is not acceptance. If a remote effect is uncertain after a timeout, the attempt remains unresolved until reconciliation; a retry requires an explicit repeat policy.

**KCC-R017.** A conforming execution adapter MUST validate evidence provenance at its declared trust boundary.

A boolean supplied by an untrusted caller saying that evidence is trusted is insufficient. The existing finite model assumes a supplied context and does not provide that production trust boundary; this limitation is measured in the linked study.

## 6. Migration and failure handling

Migration creates a new representation and retains the source revision and transformation record. Missing conditions, grants or capabilities remain unknown. A free-text blueprint is not automatically converted into a process graph.

**KCC-R018.** Unmapped fields and semantic losses MUST be reported.

Schema validity alone cannot justify `semantic_equivalence`.

**KCC-R019.** Refusal reports SHOULD identify the failed obligation and an actionable reason without exposing protected grounds to an unauthorized recipient.

**KCC-R020.** An implementation MUST NOT create a success-shaped output when assembly, authorization, or evidence checking fails.

## 7. Acceptance cases and evidence

**KCC-R021.** A conformance suite MUST include: a supported positive request; a missing required dependency; an unknown required profile; a conflicting constraint; an outdated requirement revision; an unauthorized executor; mismatched output evidence; a migration with unknown values; and a false claim of trusted evidence.

**KCC-R022.** Schema, internal consistency, applicability, and execution acceptance MUST be reported separately.

The [contract-profile study](../../studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md) reports 35/35 and 26/26 bounded scenario results, alongside false trusted-input acceptances and a 9/9 versus 9/9 baseline tie. These observations support finite conformance claims and expose the trust-boundary gap. They do not establish general task superiority or production authorization.

The scientific justification is developed in [controlled knowledge reuse](../../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md), [evidence and falsification](../../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md), and the [shared glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

# Knowledge Evolution and Versioned Rules

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

**EVO-R001.** Every new knowledge or decision artifact MUST record the exact contracts, profiles and rules that materially governed its creation.

Reassessment under later rules is a new observation. The original derivation and revision remain traceable.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). This specification governs changes to interpretation and admission rules as well as software versions. A named research direction does not by itself establish an implemented schema or executable validator. The [revision paper](../../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md) supplies the underlying model.

## 2. Creation basis

**EVO-R002.** A creation-basis record MUST identify the artifact revision, material source revisions, contract and profile definitions, relevant rule-set versions, transformation recipe, responsible actor, creation time or interval, and known gaps.

**EVO-R003.** References MUST resolve to immutable revisions or record the inability to resolve them.

Only actually used rules are included as creation inputs.

**EVO-R004.** A later adopted rule MUST NOT be inserted into the old basis as though it governed the original work.

Broad labels such as “latest contract” are insufficient when the exact revision is material.

**EVO-R005.** The implementation MUST distinguish content change from representation change, assessment change and governance change.

An updated assessment can change whether a use is admitted without modifying source bytes. A corrected interpretation produces a new semantic revision even if the original source is unchanged.

## 3. Change proposal and adoption

**EVO-R006.** Each proposal MUST identify the previous and proposed rule revisions, motivation, affected scope, expected compatibility, migration implications, evidence, objections and known unresolved questions.

Adoption requires the decision maker and authority specified by the governance policy. A contributor, editor or automated validator does not acquire that authority merely by producing a proposal.

**EVO-R007.** Conflicting adoption decisions MUST block the affected use until a decision maker with authenticated authority resolves the conflict or explicitly adopts a permissible narrower scope.

The unresolved conflict is `unknown_impact` with unknown eligibility and an `unresolved` operation outcome; a separate known prohibition yields `blocked` and refusal. The expected test result is no publication or admitted serve while the conflict is unresolved.

An adoption decision is evidence that a norm was accepted within its scope. It is not proof that every factual claim motivating that norm is true.

**EVO-R008.** The decision MUST retain links to the assessed proposal and any restrictions or effective date.

## 4. Impact analysis and reassessment

Impact analysis starts from declared dependency relations.

**EVO-R009.** Affected artifacts MUST be classified with the relevant new rule set and use scope.

The impact tokens are `compatible`, `restricted`, `requires_review`, `blocked` and `unknown_impact`, with the eligibility/decision mapping in the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies). These are projections over individual observations, not permanent global truth labels.

**EVO-R010.** A compatibility result MUST state the preservation argument or tests.

Unknown dependency coverage cannot support a claim that every affected artifact was found. Conservative re-evaluation is required when an untracked dependency could change a mandatory obligation.

Reassessment records the old artifact, new rule set, relevant current state, method, results and limitations. A passing reassessment under a narrower test does not establish compatibility with a broader profile. Multiple assessments can coexist when their scopes differ.

## 5. Migration

A migration creates a new representation or revision with an explicit source link, mapping, validator results, losses and unresolved fields.

**EVO-R011.** It MUST preserve negation, conditions, attribution and applicable constraints or declare their loss.

**EVO-R012.** Missing capability or authority MUST remain unknown.

A migration cannot manufacture an execution attempt or successful result.

**EVO-R013.** If semantic equivalence has not been established, the migration MUST state that limit even when the new structure is valid.

Reversible byte transformation establishes only the declared representation property. Lossless semantic migration needs an interpretation-preservation argument within a defined domain.

## 6. Visibility and rollback

**EVO-R014.** Publication MUST select a coherent set of artifact revisions, catalogue entries and rendered representations.

**EVO-R015.** A pointer update MUST have an identified adoption decision and the complete atomic [publication guard](../cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md#5-guarded-publication-and-serving), including current authority and dependency epochs.

**EVO-R016.** The website, downloadable corpus and API index MUST expose their actual source revisions so a consumer can detect differences.

Rollback changes the selected current head; it does not erase the later revision or its observations.

**EVO-R017.** If a rule was withdrawn because of a known defect or disclosure restriction, a rollback MUST preserve that knowledge and MUST NOT silently reauthorize a previously blocked use.

## 7. Acceptance cases

Required cases include a rule revision with no content change; an affected artifact discovered through an indirect dependency; an unresolved impact due to incomplete dependencies; an invalid migration that loses a constraint; conflicting adoption decisions; a late publisher; and rollback after revocation.

**EVO-R018.** Each case MUST retain the historical creation basis and the separate current decision.

The [projection and revision study](../../studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md) supplies bounded evidence about addressed changes and finite projections. The [conformance study](../../studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md) records limited migration checks. Their results do not establish automatic migration of arbitrary knowledge graphs or a deployed community governance service.

## 8. Publication and implementation versions

**EVO-R019.** The document edition, serialized profile, validator implementation, evidence protocol, service build and index snapshot MUST have separately identified versions.

Removing a development label from a manuscript does not change a deployed protocol. Conversely, an unfinished service feature does not prevent publication of a complete formal argument with explicit assumptions.

The [source map and compatibility record](../../../appendices/source-map/APPENDIX.md) identifies what this edition replaces and what historical wire versions remain unchanged. The [API integration appendix](../../../appendices/api-integration/APPENDIX.md) defines the observable publication boundaries without claiming an unverified contract-composition endpoint.

title: "Reuse Policy: Time, Authorization, and Decision Outcomes"
artifact: "engineering-specification"

# Reuse Policy: Time, Authorization, and Decision Outcomes

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

**RUS-R014.** A reuse policy determines whether a saved result may satisfy a new request, what MUST be rechecked, and which time and authority constraints apply.

It operates on a specific artifact, recipient, purpose, requirements revision and observation time.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language). A cache hit is only a candidate. The [compiled-artifact specification](../compiled-artifact-contract/SPECIFICATION.md) defines the saved result; [identity and invalidation](../cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md) defines its dependency and serve guards.

## 2. Admission before timing

**RUS-R001.** Before deciding freshness, the policy MUST establish semantic compatibility with the request, required dependency availability, applicable constraints, current disclosure authorization and any action-specific grant.

A fresh but inapplicable result is ineligible. A byte-identical object does not inherit the authority of another recipient.

A sufficient existing authorization is used within its scope; this specification does not require asking a user for the same permission at every pipeline step. It does require checking that the authorization still covers the actual action, subject and time.

## 3. Temporal modes

| Mode | Commitment | Continuing obligations |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Historical pinned | Describe the identified historical revision as historical | Current disclosure authorization and applicable hard restrictions |
| Current | Establish the relevant current source and policy state at the serve boundary | Dependency, scope and authority checks under the declared consistency model |
| Bounded staleness | Permit a named maximum age under an explicit policy | Hard restrictions, provenance, uncertainty and any mandatory revalidation |

**RUS-R002.** Service availability MUST NOT silently choose a weaker temporal mode.

Historical correctness does not authorize disclosure of a withdrawn or restricted record. A background refresh does not by itself make the old content eligible.

## 4. Time model and deadlines

**RUS-R003.** The policy MUST distinguish source-event time, source-observation time, artifact-build time, check time, authorization interval and storage time.

Updating a digest check does not make an old fact new. The relevant clock and uncertainty bounds belong to the observation.

For the bounded interval model, a check lies within `[checked.lower, checked.upper]` and the request time within `[now.lower, now.upper]`. The check is the policy-relevant observation, not a storage touch or unrelated digest check.

**RUS-R004.** Times MUST use one declared clock and unit.

**RUS-R005.** Bounds and durations MUST be finite; intervals require lower ≤ upper, and TTL and permitted maximum age require nonnegative values.

A TTL yields a **soft** deadline `checked.lower + ttl` unless the policy explicitly classifies that TTL as hard.

**RUS-R006.** A policy MUST identify each deadline's class and basis.

The effective hard deadline is the minimum of every applicable finite hard deadline, including hard TTL, grant expiry and a mandatory maximum-age bound. Reuse requires `now.upper < effective_hard_deadline`; equality closes the window. An explicit absence of all hard limits is not an unknown limit and does not waive other gates.

A soft deadline is reached at `now.upper >= soft_deadline`. Reaching it changes freshness to stale and requires `revalidate` before serving unless the request explicitly permits bounded stale serving. Under bounded staleness the conservative age is `now.upper - checked.lower`, anchored to that same policy-relevant check. `serve_stale` requires age strictly below the declared maximum age, no hard expiry or revocation, all other gates discharged, and a recorded refresh duty.

**RUS-R007.** A failed refresh MUST NOT reset either deadline.

**RUS-R008.** Invalid intervals (lower > upper), a definitely future check (`checked.lower > now.upper`), negative durations, incompatible clocks or unknown mandatory bounds MUST yield `unresolved` without an admitted serve.

**RUS-R009.** Where order itself is mandatory, overlapping intervals that cannot establish `checked.upper <= now.lower` also remain unresolved; an implementation MUST NOT invent a chronology from point estimates.

These interval rules do not claim distributed causal ordering.

## 5. Decision outcomes

The following tokens are **reuse decisions**, distinct from operation outcomes in the [status model](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#3-canonical-dimension-vocabularies).

| Decision | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `exact_reuse` | Serve only after every required gate and final serve guard pass within the chosen temporal mode |
| `revalidate` | Obtain current observations; no admitted serve before they discharge the gates |
| `rebuild` | Produce a new artifact under the current inputs/rules and assess it separately |
| `instantiate` | Produce and assess a new template instance with bound parameters |
| `refuse` | Report a known blocking condition at the permitted disclosure level |
| `unresolved` | Report missing, invalid or inconclusive mandatory observations; do not serve as admitted |
| `serve_stale` | Serve only under explicit bounded-staleness authorization, recording stale freshness, bound, observation basis and refresh duty |

**RUS-R010.** A stale fallback MUST NOT conceal known semantic invalidity, a hard expiry or revocation.

`serve_stale` differs from `revalidate`: the former permits delivery under a stated weaker temporal promise, the latter does not. Choosing a rebuild or refresh plan does not establish successful completion of that plan.

## 6. Cost, retention and measurement

Cost can influence an eligible choice, but cannot excuse a mandatory violation.

**RUS-R011.** A utility estimate MUST state its task, baseline, horizon, units and included costs.

**RUS-R012.** Build, retrieval, validation, maintenance and integration costs MUST NOT be counted twice.

Planned savings are estimates until measured.

Retention is a storage policy, not an eligibility policy. Popularity can justify keeping bytes longer; it does not extend the validity of their grounds. Negative results require especially explicit corpus scope and invalidation conditions.

## 7. Acceptance and evidence

Required cases include a current request with unavailable source-head evidence; a historical request after disclosure revocation; unknown clock bounds; equality at hard expiry; a soft TTL versus a hard TTL; invalid and overlapping clock bounds; a bounded-age limit measured from the relevant check; stale serving explicitly allowed and explicitly forbidden; refresh failure; a template instance with changed authority; and a low-cost option that violates a mandatory constraint.

The [reuse-admission and cost study](../../studies/reuse-admission-and-cost-controls/STUDY.md) distinguishes formal checks from measured operational savings. The [utility paper](../../../papers/knowledge-utility/PAPER.md) states conditional cost results and the assumptions needed for causal comparison. [RFC 5861](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#stale-http-responses) provides an HTTP analogy for stale handling; it is not an authorization policy for knowledge or actions.

## 8. Compatibility

No illustrative TTL is a universal default.

**RUS-R013.** A deployment MUST choose and justify its policy for the task and risk.

Importing an older record preserves its original observations and adds unknown values where the new policy needs information not present in the source. A prior all-purpose validity flag cannot be losslessly decomposed into freshness, eligibility and authority without additional evidence.

BLOB engineering/specifications/semantic-types/SPECIFICATION.md 4428c3cdacfe5e8bcf00b8d9100a79f5ce9691edecd914063698bfb3f97e8f6f original 7411

title: "Semantic Types, Attribution, and Representations"
artifact: "engineering-specification"

# Semantic Types, Attribution, and Representations

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

**SEM-R001.** This specification defines the distinctions an implementation MUST preserve when interpreting and representing knowledge.

Its facets organize content without claiming an exhaustive taxonomy of all human meaning. A procedure, a quoted procedure, and a deliberately incorrect procedural example may use similar words but support different uses.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

**SEM-R002.** A conforming profile MUST state its vocabulary, cardinalities and representation mapping.

The [scientific model](../../../papers/semantic-objects-and-context/PAPER.md) defines the separation between a source representation, interpretation, grounds and applicability.

## 2. Independent semantic dimensions

| Dimension | Information to retain |
| --- | --- |
| Representation | Text, image, audio, video, structured data or an explicit combination |
| Content kind | Definition, observation, description, model, procedure, structure, constraint, goal, argument, question or another declared kind |
| Discourse function | Explanation, instruction, quotation, illustration, counterexample, question or another contextual role |
| Aboutness | The entities, concepts or domains to which the content refers |
| Attribution | Who authored, reported, interpreted or assessed the addressed content |
| Source position | Whether a source asserts, hypothesizes, denies, asks or reports another position |
| Operators | Negation, quantification, modality, condition, exception, order and their exact scopes |
| Assessments | References to observations about fidelity, support or another stated property |
| Governance | Adoption and authority records, where relevant to the intended use |
| Applicability | Domain, time, recipient and other conditions under which the content may be used |

**SEM-R003.** A profile MAY store several dimensions in one structured record, but MUST NOT conflate their meanings.

A source position is not a truth value. An author's approval of a method is not current permission for a recipient to perform it. A generated image description is not the source image.

**SEM-R004.** For each required facet, the profile MUST distinguish known value, unknown value, justified not applicable, and withheld value.

Unknown is not an empty known list.

**SEM-R005.** Withheld indicates a disclosure boundary and MUST NOT be represented as evidence that no value exists.

## 3. Identity, granularity and relations

One source region can support several semantic objects; one semantic object can require several source regions.

**SEM-R006.** Extraction boundaries MUST follow the obligations of the intended interpretation.

A condition in a heading, a table unit or a diagram legend can be mandatory even if it falls outside a retrieved chunk.

**SEM-R007.** Source identity, semantic object identity, representation identity and retrieval chunk identity MUST remain distinguishable.

Rechunking an index need not change the interpreted semantic object. Correcting an interpretation does require a new semantic revision. A different rendering can preserve a semantic revision only when the claimed equivalence has a stated basis.

**SEM-R008.** Relations MUST identify their endpoints and kind.

A navigation link suggests further reading; a mandatory dependency says that a declared use requires the linked information.

**SEM-R009.** A vague association MUST NOT be silently upgraded to a proof premise, causal link or authority relation.

## 4. Scope and ambiguity

**SEM-R010.** Operators MUST bind to their actual scope.

“Not all members satisfy P” cannot be rewritten as “all members do not satisfy P.” An absence of permission is not an explicit prohibition, although either may prevent a particular action under its policy. A reported hypothesis is not an accepted conclusion.

**SEM-R011.** When several interpretations remain possible, an implementation MUST retain the alternatives or mark the interpretation unresolved.

Choosing one is permitted only with a recorded basis and a declaration of remaining uncertainty. A confidence score alone does not explain which interpretation was chosen or which distinction could change the answer.

## 5. Multimodal content and delivery

**SEM-R012.** A media reference MUST identify the representation revision and selector semantics.

**SEM-R013.** Required legends, ordering, coordinate conventions, units and joint components MUST be retained.

Delivery of a textual substitute is sufficient only when the task's protected observations can be established from that substitute.

OCR, a model-generated description, a human transcription and an original visual are separately attributable derivatives.

**SEM-R014.** A profile MUST record which was inspected.

Claims about transcription fidelity require a comparison method and a reference; merely preserving all generated descriptions does not measure their accuracy.

## 6. Validation obligations

**SEM-R015.** The conformance suite MUST include an attributed false assertion, a scoped negation, a quotation inside another quotation, a counterexample with tempting instructional wording, an unresolved alternative reading, and a required media dependency omitted from delivery.

The expected behavior is preservation or explicit refusal; fluent reconstruction is not a substitute for those checks.

Structural validation can check facet presence, vocabulary and references. It does not establish that the extractor found every relevant condition. Semantic fidelity therefore requires content-specific assessment and a stated inspection method.

## 7. Evidence and interoperability

The [transcription and uncertainty study](../../studies/source-transcription-and-uncertainty/STUDY.md) reports how generated descriptions and uncertainty were retained in readable projections. It explicitly separates that result from unmeasured OCR or interpretation accuracy. Domain examples belong to that engineering study.

[SKOS](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#skos) provides useful concepts, labels and relations; [PROV-DM](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#provenance) supplies provenance distinctions. This profile does not claim to replace either standard or to make their graphs prove content truth.

**SEM-R016.** Vocabulary mappings MUST state whether they are exact, broader, narrower or merely related.

See the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md) for the project's preferred terms.

BLOB engineering/specifications/status-model/SPECIFICATION.md 2f66342472fc4198eba573d7d1eda5d58d5bae9de904f901f5094037dd628d0a original 15930

title: "Assessment, Execution, and Publication States"
artifact: "engineering-specification"

# Assessment, Execution, and Publication States

**Author:** Pavel Valentov · **Publication edition:** 1.1

## 1. Purpose and conformance

This is an architectural requirement set. Interoperability requires a named representation profile; the implemented exchange subset is limited to [observations, change events and profile advertisements](../status-model/SPECIFICATION.md#8-bounded-exchange-profile), and the [book-package profile](../book-package/SPECIFICATION.md) has its own schema. No full-contract wire format or deployed runtime is implied.

A status is meaningful only for a named subject, property, revision and scope. One “verified” flag cannot faithfully represent schema validity, source fidelity, truth, coverage, authorization, execution outcome and publication review. This specification separates those questions and defines their transition obligations.

Capitalized requirements use [BCP 14](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#requirement-language).

**STA-R001.** A profile MUST select the dimensions needed for its purpose and declare any aggregation rule.

It need not attach every possible dimension to every record. The [evidence paper](../../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md) explains why stronger conclusions need different evidence.

## 2. Observation record

**STA-R002.** Each observation MUST identify its subject and immutable revision, assessed property, requirements or criterion revision, method, observer, relevant time, result, grounds and limits.

A result without a subject is not transferable evidence.

**STA-R003.** If the method trusts supplied input rather than independently authenticating it, that assumption MUST be stated.

The check result vocabulary is `pass`, `fail`, `unknown`, and `not_applicable`. The last value requires a demonstrated inapplicability rule, not missing data. For a conjunctive set of mandatory checks, any applicable failure prevents acceptance; any remaining applicable unknown prevents an all-pass conclusion.

**STA-R004.** A positive result MUST NOT overwrite a negative or unresolved result on another mandatory property.

## 3. Canonical dimension vocabularies

**STA-R005.** A profile using the current tokens MUST use the snake_case values below without overloading one dimension with another.

**STA-R006.** An older profile MAY retain its original lexical values only through an explicit, loss-reporting adapter.

| Dimension | Tokens |
| --- | --- |
| Availability | `known`, `unknown`, `not_applicable`, `withheld` |
| Coverage | `supported`, `partial`, `unsupported`, `conflicted`, `unknown` |
| Search assurance | `candidate_only`, `exhaustive_in_scope`, `incomplete_procedure` |
| Local run phase | `planned`, `ready`, `running`, `paused`, `finished` |
| Operation outcome | `success`, `partial`, `refused`, `failed`, `unresolved` |
| Stop reason | `completed`, `clarification_needed`, `budget_insufficient`, `retrieval_limit`, `stale_evidence`, `precondition_not_met`, `cancelled`, `execution_error`, `reconciliation_pending` |
| External effect | `none_established`, `known_effect`, `unknown_effect` |
| Artifact lifecycle | `building`, `ready`, `quarantined`, `retired`, `failed` |
| Residency | `resident`, `evicted`, `absent`, `unknown` |
| Freshness | `fresh`, `stale`, `expired`, `unknown` |
| Reuse eligibility | `eligible`, `ineligible`, `unknown` |
| Evolution impact | `compatible`, `restricted`, `requires_review`, `blocked`, `unknown_impact` |
| Reuse decision | `exact_reuse`, `revalidate`, `rebuild`, `instantiate`, `refuse`, `unresolved`, `serve_stale` |
| Case evidence | `planned`, `illustrated`, `executed`, `evaluated` |
| Review kind | `editorial`, `internal_technical`, `independent_agent`, `external_peer` |

`unsupported` always refers to the declared evaluated scope. `ready` describes run or artifact availability and is not a successful operation outcome. `none_established` reports no established effect within the observed scope; it does not assert that no remote effect exists. Review kind describes the actual protocol, not the name of the reviewer. An independently tasked agent review is not external human peer review.

**STA-R007.** Legacy labels MUST map explicitly: `terminated` → run phase `finished`; `unsupported in scope` → coverage `unsupported`; “incomplete execution of planned search” → `incomplete_procedure`; `not_measured` → assessment `unknown` with reason “no observation.” Profile-specific quality states such as book-package `reviewed` retain their own declared scope; they are not aliases for `pass`.

Evolution impact is an input to admission, not a shortcut to its conclusion:

| Impact | Eligibility and permitted next decision |
| --- | --- |
| `compatible` | Still `unknown` until every other mandatory gate passes; then eligible for the policy's permitted reuse |
| `restricted` | Original broader request is ineligible; a separately identified narrowed request is assessed anew |
| `requires_review` / `unknown_impact` | Unknown eligibility; `revalidate` or `unresolved`, with no admitted serve yet |
| `blocked` | Ineligible for that use; `refuse`, or a distinct permissible rebuild/instance evaluated from its own grounds |

## 4. Transition rules

Transitions apply to the same identified attempt, lifecycle instance, policy and use scope.

**STA-R008.** Implementations MUST follow the tables; transitions not listed are rejected.

Creating a new subject or attempt starts a new history rather than changing the meaning of an old record.

**STA-R009.** Every transition MUST record the triggering observation, actor, prior state and guard result.

### Execution phase

| From | Event and guard | To | Terminal? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| no attempt | Allocate a unique attempt with pinned Work Item and requirements | `planned` | no |
| `planned` | Establish inputs, capability and preconditions for dispatch | `ready` | no |
| `ready` | Recheck current authority and preconditions at actual dispatch | `running` | no |
| `running` | Record a resumable checkpoint under a declared pause policy | `paused` | no |
| `paused` | Validate checkpoint, current bindings and renewed dispatch guards | `running` | no |
| `planned` / `ready` | Cancel, refuse or fail before dispatch; record reason and actual effect observations | `finished` | yes |
| `running` / `paused` | Terminate with explicit outcome, reason and effect observations | `finished` | yes |

**STA-R010.** A finished attempt MUST NOT return to running; retry uses a new attempt with a repeat policy.

Later reconciliation adds effect observations to the same historical attempt without pretending another execution occurred. A successful process exit does not supply missing acceptance evidence.

### Artifact lifecycle

| From | Event and guard | To | Terminal? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| no lifecycle | Allocate a build record | `building` | no |
| `building` | Successful build, required checks and the atomic publication guard all pass | `ready` | no |
| `building` | Build fails; retain cause and any explicitly partial output | `failed` | yes |
| `building` / `ready` | Authorized integrity, safety or provenance investigation suppresses use | `quarantined` | no |
| `quarantined` | Authorized release after cause resolution, complete reassessment and current publication guard | `ready` | no |
| `building` / `ready` / `quarantined` | Authorized withdrawal or cancellation retires this lifecycle | `retired` | yes |

`quarantined` means use is suspended while a named issue is investigated; only the policy's authorized actor can release it. `retired` means this lifecycle is withdrawn and cannot become ready again. `failed` is also terminal for this build. A replacement or reinstatement creates a new lifecycle/adoption record and retains prior revocations and restrictions. Identical bytes do not erase those decisions.

### Freshness and eligibility projections

These are projections of immutable observations, not edits to old verdicts.

**STA-R011.** New projection records MAY replace the current display only while preserving the observations on which earlier displays were based.

| Dimension: from | Trigger and required evidence | To |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Freshness: any | A new applicable check, valid clock bounds and current policy establish a new window | `fresh` |
| Freshness: `fresh` | Soft deadline reached while no hard deadline has expired | `stale` |
| Freshness: `fresh` / `stale` | A hard deadline or permitted age bound is reached | `expired` |
| Freshness: any | Mandatory time, policy or chronology cannot be established | `unknown` |
| Eligibility: any | Every current mandatory gate for this exact request is discharged | `eligible` |
| Eligibility: any | A known mandatory restriction fails | `ineligible` |
| Eligibility: any | A material dependency, authority or assessment becomes unknown | `unknown` |

**STA-R012.** An expired or ineligible observation MUST NOT be overwritten merely because another record has a later timestamp.

Eligibility can become positive only after every known blocking cause is resolved under the current policy. Rechecking a digest alone cannot renew source-event age. The [reuse policy](../reuse-policy/SPECIFICATION.md) defines deadline and decision semantics.

Residency changes only on storage observations: storing and reading back the named bytes establishes `resident`; an eviction receipt establishes `evicted`; a scoped absence check establishes `absent`; lack of trustworthy storage observation gives `unknown`.

**STA-R013.** These transitions MUST NOT change truth, review, freshness or eligibility by themselves.

Assessment, coverage, assurance, external effect, review and case-evidence records are append-only observations. They have no automatic success progression: an illustration never becomes an executed trial without a new execution record, and an unknown effect is resolved only by evidence from the relevant effect boundary. Conflicting observations remain addressable. The monotone properties are observation history, terminal attempt/lifecycle histories and non-reused [publication generations](../cache-identity-and-invalidation/SPECIFICATION.md#5-guarded-publication-and-serving), not steadily improving truth or eligibility.

## 5. Scientific and editorial status

A completed publication can state conditional propositions and report bounded results while acknowledging unanswered empirical questions. Its edition number identifies a coherent text, not proof of all future applications.

**STA-R014.** The vocabulary MUST NOT equate “published,” “reviewed,” “implemented,” and “effective.”

**STA-R015.** An internal agent review MUST identify the reviewer, input revision, method and conclusions, and MUST be labelled as such.

It does not constitute independent external peer review. A finite test report requires actual outputs; an illustrated scenario is not an executed test.

**STA-R016.** An executed failed trial remains useful evidence and MUST retain its negative result.

## 6. User-facing projections

**STA-R017.** Interfaces MAY summarize these dimensions, but the summary MUST preserve the distinctions relevant to the user's decision.

**STA-R018.** “Indexed revision X” is more precise than an unqualified “current.” “Checks passed for the supplied record” MUST NOT become “the action is authorized” when current authorization was outside the checked boundary.

**STA-R019.** An unknown effect after a timeout MUST remain visible when a user decides whether to retry.

Technical detail can be expanded on demand, but critical uncertainty cannot be hidden behind a positive badge.

## 7. Acceptance and evidence

**STA-R020.** Tests MUST cover a passed schema with false supplied grounds, successful execution with a failed result criterion, a finished run with unknown effect, a resident but revoked artifact, a correct historical result ineligible for a current task, and an internal review that does not establish empirical validity.

The [conformance study](../../studies/contract-profile-conformance/STUDY.md) demonstrates why a trusted-input premise matters. The [editorial pilot](../../studies/editorial-skill-pilot/STUDY.md) reports an equal observed score for the structured and baseline conditions. Neither result permits a general effectiveness label. See [revision and governance](../../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md) for the distinction between an observation and adoption of a rule.

## 8. Bounded exchange profile

The [JSON Schema](../../../schemas/contract-observation.schema.json), [validator](../../../tools/check_contract_profile.py), [examples](../../../schemas/fixtures/contract-observation/valid/) and [tests](../../../tools/test_contract_profile.py) define a concrete exchange subset: `kc-observation/1`, `kc-dependency-change/1` and `kc-profile-advertisement/1`. This is an original project profile using [JSON](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259) and [JSON Schema 2020-12](https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/json-schema-core), not a claim that a full Knowledge Contract, executor or API is interoperable.

**STA-R021.** An exchange record MUST be a single strict UTF-8 JSON object without a BOM, duplicate member names at any depth, non-finite number tokens or isolated Unicode surrogates.

Unknown fields, profiles and record versions are rejected.

**STA-R022.** Integers used as epochs or sequence numbers MUST be in 0 through 9,007,199,254,740,991.

Timestamp strings use UTC seconds `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` with a valid calendar date; other clock models require another profile.

| Record | Required fields and meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `KCObservation/v1` | `observation_id`, `subject`, assessed `property`, pinned `criterion`, method `id`/`version`, `observer`, `observed_at`, check `result`, textual `scope`, `evidence_refs`, `trust_assumptions`, `limitations` |
| Subject/criterion/evidence reference | `artifact_id`, immutable `revision`, `hash_domain`, exact `sha256` |
| `not_applicable` observation | Additionally, a pinned `inapplicability_rule`; presence is structural evidence only, not proof that the rule applies |
| `KCDependencyChange/v1` | `event_id`, ordering `namespace`, `sequence`, `dependency_id`, old/new epochs and revisions, event `kind`, impact, observer and time; see the cache specification |
| `KCProfileAdvertisement/v1` | `reader_id`, explicit `read_profiles`, `write_profiles`, supported `hash_domains`; lists are capability declarations, not authorization grants |

**STA-R023.** Implementations MUST compare schema/profile support before exchanging records.

**STA-R024.** They MUST NOT infer support for an entire contract family from support for this subset.

Scope strings are exact descriptive bindings; automated semantic equivalence of two scope strings is outside this profile.

| Registered hash domain | Exact input and equality |
| --- | --- |
| `sha256/raw-bytes-v1` | SHA-256 of the exact referenced file bytes; no decoding or rewriting |
| `sha256/raw-utf8-json-v1` | SHA-256 of the exact valid exchange JSON bytes, including whitespace; no canonicalization or Unicode normalization |

**STA-R025.** Identity comparisons MUST include both domain and digest.

Reserializing an equal JSON value can change byte identity and therefore creates a new representation.

**STA-R026.** This profile deliberately does not define value-level canonical JSON hashing; a profile that needs it MUST name a separate procedure and domain.

Structural validation does not authenticate an observer, prove an observation or admit an action.
