# Contract profiles: finite conformance, trust boundaries, and a strong simple comparator

## Abstract

This study examines whether a finite implementation of a Knowledge Contract follows its declared structural and application rules. The implementer's development fixtures matched 35 of 35 expected ordinary outcomes; this is regression evidence. A separately composed control suite, prepared without inspecting the implementation but within the same research session, matched 26 of 26. These results concern already interpreted JSON instances. The implementation also accepted two author and four control inputs whose trusted annotations were deliberately false. Those acceptances expose a trust boundary and are excluded from the ordinary totals. A subsequent strong plain-form checker matched the recorded Knowledge Contract decisions on all nine shared scenarios after its author knew their inputs and expected outcomes. This shows that a direct checker can encode the tested obligations; it establishes neither generalization nor an advantage of the larger representation. The complete retained result records accompany this report.

## Question and model

The practical question is whether a consumer can distinguish an adequately bound informational package from one that omits a required condition, uses evidence for another task, or lacks a current supplied permission. The tested carrier is `kc-synthesis/0.1`. Its nine logical groups represent identity, purpose, content, scope, grounds, dependencies, obligations, projection, and observations. Optional versioned profiles add professional work, dialogue, documents, reasoning, visual delivery, and reuse.

A profile extends the obligations of the core. It does not override them by appearing later in a file. If admission requires the core condition \(C_0\) and applicable profile conditions \(C_1,\ldots,C_n\), then

\[
\operatorname{Admit}(x) \Rightarrow \bigwedge_{i=0}^{n} C_i(x)
\Rightarrow C_0(x).
\]

This implication follows from the admission definition. Whether software implements that definition correctly is a separate question. Whether an external annotation is true is another. The [conditional-properties record](evidence/conditional-properties-source.json) states these distinctions and supplies counterexamples. Their theoretical setting is developed in [Evidence and falsification](../../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md) and [Constrained transformations](../../../papers/constrained-transformations/PAPER.md).

The implementation assumes that objects and relationships have already been interpreted. A supplied capability annotation, source availability, and a current authority decision are inputs. The checker does not independently interview an actor, authenticate an external grant, or establish a source's factual accuracy. An informational contract may omit the professional profile; an execution request has additional binding requirements. Unknown required profiles block use. Unknown optional profiles can be retained as opaque data with a diagnostic, which does not count as interpreting them.

## Design and unit of observation

The [evaluation protocol](evidence/evaluation-protocol-source.json) distinguishes a scenario from assertions nested inside it. The author prepared the interface and its own suite. A different agent in the same session prepared control intents before inspecting the implementation. After receiving the interface, that agent fixed concrete cases, expected outcomes, and a runner before its first import of the validator implementation. The retained [control review](evidence/control-review-source.json) describes this sequence.

There were 28 initial control intents: 24 ordinary and four trust-boundary stress intents. Interface mapping produced 26 ordinary concrete scenarios and four stress scenarios. The 26 comprise six expected positive outcomes, seventeen expected rejections, and three migration-preservation cases. Only four of the six positive outcomes are application admissions. One intent could not be mapped and five were only partially represented. These omissions remain part of the scope assessment; they are not additional passed cases.

| Operation in the retained control report | Ordinary cases | Expected positive outcomes | Expected rejections | Preservation-only cases |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Application assessment | 13 | 4 | 9 | 0 |
| Execution verification | 6 | 1 | 5 | 0 |
| Profile compatibility | 2 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
| Contract validation | 2 | 0 | 2 | 0 |
| Legacy migration | 3 | 0 | 0 | 3 |
| Total | 26 | 6 | 17 | 3 |

These are different predicates: a valid schema, an accepted execution, a compatible profile and permission for an application are not interchangeable decisions. The table is recomputed from the report's `operation` and expected-check fields. The four application positives are C01, C02, C06B and C18; C02B concerns execution, and C12 concerns compatibility.

The control composition is organizational separation within one session and agent family. It is not an external replication or independent domain review. Cases were deliberately selected to exercise mechanisms, so their proportions do not estimate error rates in a population of real tasks.

## Results

| Evaluation | Ordinary expected outcomes matched | False trusted inputs accepted | Meaning of the result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Author development fixtures | 35/35: 13 expected positives, 22 expected rejections | 2/2 | Regression evidence; separately disclosed trust-boundary stress |
| Separate control suite | 26/26 | 4/4 | Exact outcomes on the frozen concrete control cases |
| Strong plain-form comparison | 9/9 for each representation | Outside this comparison | Equal decisions on three allowed and six forbidden admissions |

These counts come from the exact [author report](evidence/author-conformance-report.json), [control report](evidence/separate-control-report.json), and [strong-comparator report](evidence/strong-plain-form-comparison.json). The reports retain input hashes, case rows, expected outcomes, and observations. Their bytes were recovered from the retained publication reader and checked against its source index; [origin.json](evidence/origin.json) records the identities and hashes.

Positive controls matter. A checker that rejects every input would avoid forbidden admissions but fail useful cases. The six positive control outcomes include a compact informational core, correctly bound work, exact synthetic execution evidence, a current grant despite a different revoked grant, an opaque optional profile, and an eligible reuse instance. Negative cases include missing capability, a mismatched or expired grant, changed requirements, absent declared imagery, unknown required profiles, lost conditions, and evidence bound to another attempt or output.

The migration cases concern a declared six-field legacy subset. They check retained original values, explicit gaps, and the absence of invented capability or authority. They do not demonstrate lossless migration of every previous contract implementation.

## Counterexamples and the comparator

The control stress cases deliberately supplied four internally consistent falsehoods: a positive capability annotation, a fabricated grant, false subject content, and an incorrect claim that a source was available. All four were admitted. This is a counterexample to the proposition that passing structural and binding checks establishes real competence, authorization, factual truth, or production access enforcement. It is not a successful semantic test merely because the interface's expected answer was acceptance.

Two comparators must also remain distinct. The initially registered diagnostic ablation checked field presence and simple form but omitted the cross-object conditions under investigation. It admitted all nine shared inputs, including all six forbidden ones; it therefore matched only the three expected positives, whereas the contract matched all nine. Its separate calibration rejected an empty task, but that input is outside the shared nine-case denominator. This establishes the effect of removing relational checks in that code. It is not a fair estimate of an advantage over a strong task form.

The subsequent strong checker received the same material information and directly checked current sources, work and requirements revisions, actor assignment, conditions, grants, and required capabilities. It used fixed fields rather than an extensible ontology and profile registry. It matched all nine expected outcomes, exactly as the stored contract run did. The comparator author knew the nine inputs and their expected outcomes from the first results. Equality is therefore an existence demonstration that a direct checker can encode these obligations, not a held-out test of generalization. Only the comparator was newly executed in this comparison; the contract side used its preserved first result. A later comparative evaluation needs unseen cases for both implementations.

The equality directly challenges a claim that the larger representation is necessary for these nine decisions. It leaves open different questions about exchange, maintenance, additional profiles, preparation effort, and scale. None of those outcomes was measured here.

## Claim, test, counterexample, and limit

| Claim | Model or test | Counterexample or rival explanation | Supported conclusion and limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Profile composition preserves core obligations | Conjunctive admission and required-profile cases | An implementation silently overrides a core condition | The defined rule preserves the core; the finite suite checks selected implementations of it |
| Evidence must refer to the attempted work | Exact task, requirements, actor, attempt, output, and criterion bindings | Old evidence has a similar description but a different target | Tested mismatches are rejected; forged trusted bindings remain outside authentication |
| A skill description establishes capability | Separate description and capability inputs | A false capability annotation is accepted | The description alone is insufficient; input truth still needs an external basis |
| Contract conformance implies source truth | Schema and consistency checks | The admitted false-source stress case | This implication is refuted within the test design |
| The contract outperforms a strong simple form | Nine shared decisions | The direct checker makes the same decisions | No advantage is established on this shared domain |

## Reproduction and evidence boundaries

The accompanying files are sufficient to inspect all recorded results, separate the stress denominator, inspect the comparator decisions, and verify the provenance of retained JSON. The exact original runners, the thirty concrete control inputs and their oracle, the strong comparator code, and the frozen implementation are not included. This is an unresolved recovery and publication gap for project-authored synthetic material, not a third-party-book rights restriction. The locally available later implementation has a different hash from the one bound to this report and cannot substitute for it. Consequently this folder supplies inspectable retained reports rather than a complete historical rerun. Hashes identify artifacts; they do not replace missing code, authenticate an observer, or certify a result's meaning. The [recovery inventory](../historical-recovery-gaps.json) lists the exact missing identities and the evidence used to identify them.

The review did recover the exact [historical `kc-synthesis/0.1` schema](historical-recovery/contract-synthesis-0.1.schema.json), whose SHA-256 agrees with the author report. It is published separately, without changing the retained evidence inventory. Recovering this one input does not recover the missing implementation, concrete fixtures or runners and does not imply conformance to the current architecture.

Rendered prose sources are explicitly identified as text views recovered from the retained HTML. Their original Markdown source hashes are recorded separately from the hashes of those new views. They are not misrepresented as byte-identical Markdown reconstructions.

The retained conditional-properties, protocol and review narratives are in Russian; JSON keys are mainly English, with some Russian descriptions. The model, design and boundary paragraphs above are English summaries of the cited passages, including the same-session review scope and unmapped controls. They are summaries, not a certified translation of every source line. The [evidence language index](../evidence-language-index.json) identifies the language and preserved hash of each distributed file.

## Conclusion

The tested profile performed its declared finite structural and relational checks, including useful positive controls and prohibited admissions. The accepted false trusted inputs demonstrate why source admission, current authority, and semantic assessment need their own evidence. The strong-comparator tie further narrows the contribution: this study supplies a working representation and inspectable finite results, while leaving comparative practical benefit unestablished. These distinctions are prerequisites for a usable contract service, not unfinished text that should be concealed by an undifferentiated success label. Terminology is consolidated in the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

# Dependency closure, readable artifact layouts, and a pinned package replay

## Abstract

A task package is useful only if selecting an artifact also delivers its required conditions and dependencies. This study examines that obligation in a frozen editorial knowledge package with 110 records and twelve skill roots. Its retained selftest matched 44 ordinary checks: 21 expected acceptances and 23 expected rejections. Two additional structural-boundary counterexamples were accepted as designed and are reported separately. The dependency portion exhaustively examined all 4,096 skill subsets and 24,576 one-step inclusion pairs, with no disagreement against a separate fixed-point implementation. A public replay reproduces that graph portion without distributing the source book. A later metadata calculation shows that the actual editorial request delivered 108 of 110 records, so the observed package does not demonstrate a small context. A separate migration report records preservation of all 110 payloads, and a pinned historical replay reproduced an unresolved preliminary decision. The results establish bounded graph, layout, and integration behavior, not semantic completeness or agent competence.

## The delivery problem

An editorial skill can depend on a rule, a condition under which that rule applies, an acceptance criterion, and a source fragment. Selecting only the top-level skill description can omit the qualification that makes its use appropriate. The consumer follows explicitly required dependencies and treats navigation separately. Avoiding arbitrary navigation is a design motivation; it is not evidence that this package is selective or economical. The retained package was derived from the supplied OCR representation of Ilyakhov and Sarycheva's *Пиши, сокращай*, identified in the [editorial-source note](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#editorial-input).

This study distinguishes three identities: the artifact's stable identity, the immutable content revision, and its human-readable path. A renamed folder need not create different knowledge. A changed condition cannot be dismissed as a cosmetic rename. The scientific obligations are developed in [Retrieval and context composition](../../../papers/retrieval-and-context-composition/PAPER.md), [Constrained transformations](../../../papers/constrained-transformations/PAPER.md), and [Revision and governance](../../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md).

## Model and algorithms

Let \(V\) be the fixed finite set of artifact records and \(D(v)\) the required dependencies of record \(v\). For selected roots \(S\), the intended closure is the least set \(C(S)\) satisfying

\[
S\subseteq C(S), \qquad v\in C(S)\Rightarrow D(v)\subseteq C(S).
\]

Only declared `required` groups with the `all` operator participate in this tested profile. An alternative-choice dependency would need another selection policy and is not silently interpreted as `all`. A dependency's identity, revision, expected type, and payload hash are checked when loading the original package. Unknown roots and cycles cause explicit rejection in the consumer.

The production-side algorithm performs depth-first traversal with separate active and completed sets. The reference implementation repeatedly computes

\[
S_{k+1}=S_k\cup\bigcup_{v\in S_k}D(v)
\]

until the set stops growing. On a finite graph, the latter terminates after at most \(|V|\) strict increases. The comparison examines agreement, idempotence, monotonicity, and the union equation for this declared dependency relation. These are graph properties; they do not state that the natural-language content has a complete semantic dependency model.

## Exhaustive finite checks

Twelve selectable skill roots yield \(2^{12}=4,096\) subsets. The selftest evaluates every subset, compares consumer closure with the separate reference, checks \(C(C(S))=C(S)\), and checks that the closure equals the union of the individual root closures. It also examines every pair formed by adding one absent skill. There are \(12\cdot2^{11}=24,576\) such pairs; each must satisfy inclusion of the smaller closure in the larger one.

The retained [finite-package selftest](evidence/finite-package-selftest.json) records no reference disagreements, idempotence failures, union failures, or monotonicity failures. Union, idempotence and monotonicity are consequences of least closure; the subset sweep mainly checks their implementation on this fixed adjacency relation, rather than supplying thousands of distinct semantic observations. These counts are not independent samples of tasks and cannot be converted into a probability that arbitrary knowledge is complete.

The historical file contains 46 rows: 44 ordinary checks (21 expected acceptances and 23 expected rejections) and two accepted trust-boundary counterexamples. The latter are excluded from ordinary conformance, following the [common counting convention](../README.md#counting-and-interpretation). Additional controls include stale review bindings, a changed protected number, missing change grounds, a wrong source relation, an omitted critical qualification, another task's context, stale competency dependencies, attempted execution without supplied capability and authority, package tampering, and immutable receipt behavior. A synthetic positive review fixture is explicitly labeled as synthetic; it is not evidence that a real reviewer judged the text.

Two intentionally passing boundaries are especially informative. A structurally valid rule can state a false proposition. An output can contain the exact protected numeric string while negating its meaning. The checker accepted those inputs in the respective structural and literal-preservation checks. Therefore neither structural conformance nor exact string presence is a substitute for semantic review.

## A public reproduction of the graph result

The [dependency graph](evidence/dependency-graph.json) publishes required adjacency data and opaque vertex identities, with no artifact bodies, source passages, OCR, or book images. It was extracted after the original loader checked the frozen package's referenced records. Its source graph, index, and loader hashes are recorded in the file. The supplied [replay program](evidence/replay_closure.py) uses the original traversal rule and a separately written fixed-point comparison in the same publication workflow. This is algorithmic separation, not independent external replication.

From this study directory, run:

```bash
python3 evidence/replay_closure.py
```

The [saved public replay](evidence/public-closure-replay.json) reproduces 4,096 subset comparisons and 24,576 inclusion checks, and rejects an unknown root and a constructed cycle. This is a new replay of the extracted historical graph. It does not pretend to rerun all 46 historical checks, validate the source book, or observe a new model response. No additional Python packages or network calls are required.

## Delivered record counts and fault controls

The least-fixed-point background and the limited role of program mutation are identified in the [methodological references](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#evaluation-methods). The measurements below concern this retained finite graph and these declared faults.

The [post-review graph check](checks/check_closure_size_and_mutations.py) calculates closure sizes from the same 110-vertex, 405-edge graph. Its [saved output](checks/closure-size-and-mutation-check.json) includes every skill, both roles, both blueprints and the top-level agent root. Counts describe records, not bytes, tokens or attention cost. Human-readable names come from the retained path migration; the historical IDs remain in the machine result for traceability.

| Selected roots | Delivered records |
| --- | ---: |
| Individual skill roots | 45–57 out of 110 (40.9–51.8%) |
| All twelve skills together | 102 |
| Main editor role | 105 |
| Review role | 57 |
| Editing blueprint | 73 |
| Top-level assistant | 110 |
| Editorial pilot: editing blueprint plus both roles | 108 (98.2%) |

The twelve single-skill counts in historical selector order are 45, 57, 45, 49, 53, 51, 51, 47, 49, 55, 53 and 45; the output maps each count to a readable name. The pilot declared five applied skills out of twelve delivered. This is no demonstration that dependency closure produces a compact task context. It establishes delivery of the declared closure; whether dependency granularity and role-level roots can reduce package size remains unmeasured.

The same new check tests three explicit implementation mutations: omitting cycle rejection, skipping a required child, and treating an unknown root as a leaf. A two-node cycle, a three-node chain and an unknown-root control distinguish all three mutants from the unchanged traversal. These are post-review synthetic fault controls, separate from the historical 44 ordinary checks and two boundary counterexamples. They show sensitivity to those three defects, not general mutation adequacy.

```bash
python3 checks/check_closure_size_and_mutations.py
```

## Readable layout migration

The later editorial toolkit layout replaced code-like filenames with descriptive paths while retaining the underlying artifact identities and payload revisions. The [preservation report](evidence/layout-preservation-report.json) records the migration tool's assertion of byte equality for all 110 records and unchanged source bytes. Each row stores one hash and `bytes_equal: true`, not two separately observed old/new hashes. Thus the report alone cannot independently re-establish both byte sequences. Old and new paths are listed explicitly, so a reader can distinguish reported relocation from revision; the original report is preserved without adding invented observations.

The [layout conformance report](evidence/layout-conformance-report.json) records nine successful tests. Its positive inventory includes 110 document passports, 110 records, twenty operational closures, four constraints, 163 Markdown files, 1,294 local links, and 24 adapters. These are different inventory units and are not added into a single success rate. Other checks examine alias equivalence, forty source locators, and delivery of constraints as critical content.

Negative controls remove a constraint passport, remove a required constraint from delivery, introduce an invalid slug, break a link while updating its file hash, and create disagreement between the index and registry. Each is rejected by the expected diagnostic family. The updated-hash broken-link control is a useful counterexample: internally consistent checksums do not imply usable navigation.

## Historical package replay

An integration gate was pinned to the earlier package, not to the later layout. Reusing that gate against changed folder membership would compare different targets. The retained [historical replay](evidence/historical-package-replay.json) instead used the exact earlier target manifest and [declared environment](evidence/historical-replay-environment.json). The five command exits matched their expected sequence: 0, 0, 0, 3, 0. The source and temporary replay copy each retained all 239 checked payload files unchanged.

The exit of 3 belonged to preliminary admission. Its expected decision was `needs_resolution`, with `R_EXPECTED_HEAD` as the resolution code. Reproducing that decision means the gate reproduced the specified unresolved condition. It does not mean the package was accepted by a server. The record explicitly reports no new model runs, no new text outcomes, no external semantic review, server admission not evaluated, and production readiness false.

The recorded environment was Python 3.12.14 on Linux with five exact dependency versions. That environment claim belongs to the pinned historical replay. The later layout also has dependencies with version ranges; matching the earlier environment does not establish an exact later environment.

## Claim, evidence, and boundary

| Claim | Evidence | Counterexample or failure control | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Required closure follows the declared graph | Exhaustive subset replay and separately implemented fixed point | Unknown root, cycle and three new implementation mutants | Completeness of the declared graph is an external premise |
| The migration tool reports unchanged content | 110 equality assertions, with one hash per row | Layout/index disagreement | Both old and new byte sequences are not separately pinned in that report |
| Constraints are actually delivered | Explicit critical-constraint checks | Missing passport and missing delivery | Only the four declared constraints are covered |
| A matching package hash means the package is usable | Navigation and preliminary-admission checks | Broken link with updated hash; unresolved expected head | Integrity, navigation, and admission remain separate |
| Exact strings imply preserved meaning | Deliberately negative meaning with the same numeric string | The literal check accepts it | A semantic reviewer remains necessary |

## Conclusion

This study provides a reproducible finite dependency result and inspectable layout and package-replay records. It shows how readable artifact names can coexist with stable identity, and how constraints can remain visible in the delivered closure. It also preserves the failure boundaries that make these results interpretable: a complete graph traversal may follow an incomplete annotation; almost the whole package may be delivered; an intact package may require resolution; a string-preserving edit may change meaning. The [editorial pilot](../editorial-skill-pilot/STUDY.md) evaluates actual text separately. Definitions are collected in the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

The original report identities remain in [origin.json](evidence/origin.json). The evidence is mostly English-keyed JSON, with retained Russian descriptions where present; its [language index](../evidence-language-index.json) identifies individual files. The prose above supplies English summaries of the false-rule and reversed-meaning controls. The newly generated graph measurements and mutation outcomes are identified separately in the [correction record](../review-corrections.json).

name: "editorial-skill-pilot"
title: "Editing a scientific section with a knowledge toolkit: a masked single-section comparison"
artifact: "engineering-study"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"
license: "CC-BY-4.0"
description: "One preregistered editorial comparison, exact initial outputs, masked review and an observed tie."
study_type: "paired-editorial-process-pilot"

# Editing a scientific section with a knowledge toolkit: a masked single-section comparison

## Abstract

Two agents edited the same 804-word Russian scientific section. Both received the common brief, shared context, source section, protected-claim registry and scoring rubric. The assisted condition additionally used an editorial toolkit containing skills and a process blueprint. The shared checklist contained nineteen protected claims, including eight conceptual distinctions, and exact literals. A separate agent reviewed anonymized outputs before their conditions were revealed. Both complied with the supplied claim checklist, passed the editorial acceptance gate, and received eight points out of ten; preference was a tie. A technical audit found a mechanically consistent trace from assisted changes to declared rules and source ranges, with limitations in how specifically some rules supported the edit. The pilot demonstrates an operational editing and traceability process on one real section. It does not establish unaided meaning preservation, better writing than the shared structured brief, a causal effect of individual skills, or scientific validity of the text.

## Question and scope

The user's practical question was whether newly created writing skills and a blueprint could guide an agent through a real publication task while preserving meaning. The selected unit was the complete working-definitions section of the project introduction: 804 words by whitespace counting, four subsections, and a table with eight distinctions. The input was Russian, so this pilot does not evaluate English translation quality. The toolkit was derived from Maxim Ilyakhov and Lyudmila Sarycheva’s *Пиши, сокращай*; the [bibliographic and input-identity note](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#editorial-input) identifies the work and the supplied representation without redistributing it.

The trial separated editorial quality from truth of the underlying scientific content. An editor could improve references, paragraph structure, and sentence clarity without establishing the correctness of a formal claim. The relevant scientific framing appears in [Constrained transformations](../../../papers/constrained-transformations/PAPER.md), [Evidence and falsification](../../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md), and [Knowledge utility](../../../papers/knowledge-utility/PAPER.md).

The exact [source section](evidence/source-section.txt), [shared context](evidence/source-context.txt), [common brief](evidence/common-editorial-brief.txt), and [original protocol](evidence/original-pilot-protocol.txt) are retained as historical text records. Their internal paths and terminology describe the original experiment; the present publication's navigation is provided by this report.

## Design and preregistration

The [preregistration](evidence/editorial-preregistration.json) predates the editor agents. It pins the inputs, identifies two conditions, specifies one first completed response per condition, and requires review with condition labels hidden until the written evaluation. The [protected-claim registry](evidence/protected-claims.json) contains nineteen claims, protected headings and anchors, link targets, numbers, versions, and 37 exact literals. First-response status is the editor's declaration: the final bytes are hash-pinned, but no independent complete event log verifies that status, rereading before registration, or the absence of edits after self-review.

Both agents received the exact section, common brief, shared context and protected-claim registry in fresh task contexts. The brief disclosed the full scoring procedure, including five axes and the meanings of scores 0, 1 and 2. The baseline edited without reading the toolkit; the assisted condition additionally used the readable-layout package, five declared applied skills, and the process blueprint for editing existing text. The registry itself is a structured statement of obligations. The comparison therefore isolates the added toolkit and compiler route relative to a common structured brief, not a brief-only condition against a contract. Preservation counts measure compliance with a supplied checklist rather than discovery or unaided preservation of unstated meaning.

After the first responses were saved, another agent received the source and variants labeled alpha and beta without the editing logs or condition mapping. It issued the [masked review](evidence/masked-editorial-review.json). The subsequent [label reveal](evidence/condition-label-reveal.json) records alpha as baseline and beta as toolkit-assisted and states that review was completed before reveal. This is masking of condition labels, not proof that the reviewer could not infer a method from stylistic differences. All roles operated within the same agent environment; there was no independent human editorial panel.

## Results on the retained responses

| Observation | Common-brief baseline | Toolkit-assisted editing |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Protected claims preserved | 19/19 | 19/19 |
| Conceptual table distinctions preserved, nested within protected claim 11 | 8/8 | 8/8 |
| Exact protected literals present | 37/37 | 37/37 |
| Headings / anchors / link destinations | 5 / 5 / 3 | 5 / 5 / 3 |
| Sentence clarity, 0–2 | 2 | 2 |
| References and terminology, 0–2 | 2 | 2 |
| Coherence, 0–2 | 2 | 2 |
| Economy without loss, 0–2 | 1 | 1 |
| Scientific genre and reader, 0–2 | 1 | 1 |
| Total assigned by the reviewer | 8/10 | 8/10 |
| Words by whitespace | 830 | 820 |
| Editorial decision | Accepted within the declared scope | Accepted within the declared scope |

The [comparison result](evidence/editorial-comparison-results.json), exact [baseline response](evidence/baseline-edit.txt), and exact [assisted response](evidence/skill-assisted-edit.txt) accompany the report. The original [mechanical check](evidence/editorial-mechanical-checks.json) verifies hashes, literal presence, and document structure. The semantic preservation counts and stylistic scores come from the recorded agent review, not the mechanical checker.

The eight distinctions are included in the nineteen claims, not eight additional independent obligations. Both score vectors were `(2, 2, 2, 1, 1)`: the first three axes were at their ceiling, and the 0–2 scale was coarse. A difference smaller than one score step on an axis would not be resolved by this instrument. Equality of totals is a result under this rubric, not evidence of identical writing quality.

The review used identical `gate_reasoning` text for both variants, so that paragraph is templated reasoning rather than two independently developed arguments. The record also contains three examined issues per variant. Their English summaries are:

| Variant and issue | What the reviewer examined | Recorded disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Baseline: ALPHA-STYLE-01 | A list of four distinctions is repeated when each is explained | Non-blocking redundancy; reflected in the economy score |
| Baseline: ALPHA-WORDING-01 | A phrase about non-identity became a phrase about lack of implication | Accepted within the supplied checklist's local meaning; no new formal precision claimed |
| Baseline: ALPHA-SOURCE-01 | Both historical Work Item spellings remain | Inherited ambiguity, not charged as a new defect |
| Assisted: BETA-SCOPE-01 | The working-profile qualifier moved before role, skill and blueprint together | No protected claim of unconditional scope was identified; no loss established within the brief |
| Assisted: BETA-STYLE-01 | A dense contract definition remains long | Non-blocking retained limitation |
| Assisted: BETA-SOURCE-01 | Both historical Work Item spellings remain | Inherited ambiguity, not charged as a new defect |

These dispositions expose the local judgment behind “preserved”; they do not settle a broader scientific interpretation that the checklist did not specify.

Both responses were slightly longer than the original. Reduction in word count was not the declared target. Replacing compressed references with explicit nouns can increase length while reducing the reader's effort to reconstruct a reference. Consequently this trial supplies no general rule that successful editing must make a text shorter.

## What the edits changed

The assisted response made some list references explicit. Instead of asking the reader to connect the first, second, and third items to an earlier list, it named source context, interaction context, and application context directly. It also replaced a vague plural referent with the already defined object being described. These changes clarified existing relationships without adding another theoretical claim.

The section discussing a contract was divided into thematic paragraphs addressing the contract and package, version history, work objects, and the boundary of the research. Some technically dense material was preserved because its distinctions were protected. The masked reviewer identified local strengths in both variants rather than a consistent overall advantage. A repeated list remained in the baseline; a long contract definition remained in the assisted version. Neither was repaired after comparison to manufacture a better first-response result.

## Skill use and technical trace

The assisted route went through a brief, request, compiled Knowledge Contract, proposed edit, review, and registration. Five skills were declared applied: defining the editing task, precise rewording, preserving numbers and conditions, structuring sentences and paragraphs, and reviewing the result with its source trace. Required dependencies caused twelve skills and 108 records to be delivered. The other seven skills were not counted as applied merely because they were loaded.

The separate [technical audit](evidence/editorial-trace-audit.json) records twelve groups of material changes, twenty change grounds, and seven process grounds. It checked 49 unique input/output locators and 24 unique source ranges. Quotes, character and UTF-8 offsets, and line bindings matched, and all changed non-whitespace characters were covered by the recorded trace. It also checked agreement among request, input, contract, selected context, output, assessment, and receipt bindings. The audit read fifteen source fragments covering nine cited rules. For the precision rule K10, making pronoun references explicit was an editorial application of a general principle, not a separately stated method in the book. The trace is mechanically consistent, while some changes have only general rule support. No visual PDF verification occurred, and the source OCR questions K02/F02 and K03/F03 remained open.

These observations establish consistency and inspectability of the particular trace. They do not reveal every internal action of the editor or prove that a cited rule caused a wording choice. The edited scientific section is the project's own text and is distributed here in full. The private third-party source is the writing guide underlying the toolkit's rules; its cited ranges and the private source-derived toolkit are not redistributed. The published audit reports those rule-source checks with a pinned source identity, rather than supplying the guide itself.

## Counterarguments and boundaries

One rival explanation is that the common brief, context, explicit checklist and disclosed rubric already supplied what this section needed. Another is that the baseline model may already have known the general editing method or similar principles through prior training; the experiment did not measure or exclude such exposure. The original protocol expressly avoided assuming that the model lacked general editing knowledge. Both explanations are compatible with the observed tie. A useful subsequent test would include project-specific rules unavailable in the common brief, a held-out task set, and a more discriminating or pairwise-preference assessment. The present result does not prove that the toolkit is useless, but cannot support a superiority claim.

A second counterargument is that a complete-looking trace may accompany an incorrect edit. Mechanical equality, literal presence, and a consistent receipt do not establish meaning preservation. The [package-delivery study](../dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md) supplies an explicit negative example in which the protected numeric string remains while the sentence's meaning is negated. The editorial review addresses that distinct question here, within one section and one reviewer's judgment.

A third boundary concerns terminology. At the time of the pilot, the editors recorded an ambiguity between two spellings of the work-item entity rather than silently deciding that they denoted different things. The author subsequently clarified that the spellings refer to one entity. The present [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md) uses that decision. Frozen pilot inputs and scores retain their historical bytes; they are not retroactively rewritten as if the clarification preceded the experiment.

There was one section and one retained response per condition, each declared first-completed by its editor. Scores are qualitative rubric judgments, not calibrated population measurements. The trial did not record token usage, cost, or latency. It did not isolate the contribution of each skill, the blueprint, or the surrounding compiler. Native command-line clients were not used. The underlying scientific claims and all referenced literature were not revalidated by this editorial exercise. The methodological distinctions between preregistered and post-hoc work, and between masked labels and a validated judge, are discussed in the [methodological references](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#evaluation-methods).

## Public record checks

The supplied [replay checker](evidence/replay_editorial_checks.py) rechecks the public retained texts against the frozen source hash, original response hashes, exact literals, headings, anchor order, and link destinations:

```bash
python3 evidence/replay_editorial_checks.py
```

The [saved check](evidence/public-editorial-record-check.json) confirms those mechanical properties. It deliberately does not compute semantic fidelity or reproduce the eight-point ratings. Complete model-runtime identity and the full private toolkit source closure are not supplied, so executing this checker is not a new agent trial. [origin.json](evidence/origin.json) pins the distributed records and distinguishes original data from newly generated check results.

The source section, context, brief, protocol, edits and substantive review prose are Russian; JSON field names are mainly English. The descriptions of the two conditions, the six examined issues and the audit qualifications above are English summaries of the cited Russian passages, not a full independently reviewed translation. The [evidence language index](../evidence-language-index.json) identifies every file without rewriting its frozen bytes.

## Conclusion

The assisted editorial process worked on the selected real section: it produced an accepted text and an inspectable chain from task to changes and review. The baseline also worked, with the same protected-claim result and score. This completed pilot supports a narrow operational claim about usable process and traceability. A claim of better writing, lower cost, or improved scientific correctness requires a different and adequately observed comparison. The present result remains valuable without changing the tie into an advantage.

BLOB engineering/studies/executable-projections-and-revision/STUDY.md 6242027572df695e1907e77d55588ea3b3a64741407b6922b87f0612de956ed8 original 17488
---
name: "executable-projections-and-revision"
title: "Executable projections, addressable revisions, and the limits of graph minimality"
artifact: "engineering-study"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
language: "en"
license: "CC-BY-4.0"
description: "Finite consumer checks, paired agent tasks, addressed revision and the limits of graph minimality."
study_type: "finite-consumer-and-agent-case-study"
review_state: "internally-reviewed"

# Executable projections, addressable revisions, and the limits of graph minimality

## Abstract

This report synthesizes three completed development stages: packet reading, targeted revision, and executable partial extraction. The first reader stage recorded twelve criterion violations and a reconstruction-status distribution of ten complete, 38 partial, three rejected and six unsupported main-layer results. A later development and regression boundary suite satisfied 772 declared checks across 38 calls under a revised reader and rubric. A targeted revision comparison produced equal outcomes on ten case templates instantiated for three books, yielding thirty graph-versus-row pairs. A consumer checked on 102 finite controls then extracted 24 prescribed projections from ten fresh agent responses organized as five related case pairs. Both representations succeeded on all five primary cases under a coarse rubric that reached its ceiling on the rated projections. These bounded results do not establish graph superiority or general semantic completeness. A post-hoc finding shows that a minimal set of reachable entities can still contain unnecessary claims inside entity text.

## Question and design progression

The engineering question is whether a consumer can retrieve a useful partial result without silently dropping its conditions, treating a new assessment as original provenance, or resolving an ambiguous identity by arbitrary order. The scientific arguments are developed in [Retrieval and context composition](../../../papers/retrieval-and-context-composition/PAPER.md), [Revision and governance](../../../papers/revision-and-governance/PAPER.md), and [Evidence and falsification](../../../papers/evidence-and-falsification/PAPER.md).

The stages changed interfaces, mappings, cases, and sometimes evaluation rules. They are therefore reported as related development studies. Their successive scores do not constitute a controlled estimate of improvement caused by one isolated intervention. Historical results remain unchanged.

## Initial reader: four distinct failure mechanisms

The retained [initial reader study](evidence/historical-reader-study-source.json) records 57 main-layer calls with 786 satisfied and twelve violated criteria. Their reconstruction statuses were ten complete, 38 partial, three rejected and six unsupported. These status labels are not interchangeable with satisfaction of all obligations. The public summary does not bind every main-layer status to a predeclared expected status, so it cannot establish which of the 38 partial and nine rejected/unsupported outcomes were planned rather than unexpected. That distinction remains unavailable at the case level.

The separate [book-layer report](../source-transcription-and-uncertainty/evidence/historical-ocr-reader-report.json) records eighteen calls with 252 satisfied and zero violated criteria: six base cases were complete and twelve deliberately damaged or incomplete controls were partial by design. Calls, reconstruction statuses and nested criteria are separate denominators; the criterion counts are not 1,050 independent tasks or an accuracy estimate. There were no native-model subject trials or human-subject trials in that stage.

The twelve violations fell into four groups. Five concerned the difference between an address resolving to a placeholder and content actually being available. Three concerned inconsistent derivation of revision identity between records and relation endpoints. Two concerned diagnostic pointers into a packet versus its transport envelope. Two concerned choosing the first candidate despite conflicting records.

| Original failure group | Recorded violations | Subsequent correction scope |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Placeholder address versus available content | 5 | Contract/rubric distinction and reader change: separate address resolution from content state and availability |
| Inconsistent inferred revisions | 3 | Reader normalization and an explicit inference rule applied consistently to records and endpoints |
| Ambiguous pointer coordinate space | 2 | Diagnostic output and contract clarification: identify packet/envelope space and representation hash |
| First-candidate choice under conflict | 2 | Resolver behavior and output contract: retain the candidate set and explicit ambiguity |

This is an English summary of the original report's four-group analysis. It does not allocate each later success causally to code or rubric alone; both changed, particularly for the five placeholder cases.

This analysis matters because the violations do not all mean lost content. In the pointer cases, the invalid outer hash was detected, but the diagnostic coordinate system was ambiguous. In the placeholder cases, relationships were retained while the meaning of `resolved` differed between reader and oracle. Revising the explanation of these mechanisms does not erase the recorded violations.

## Revised reader boundaries

The later reader separated address resolution, content state, content availability, applicability, and authority. An empty supplied string, zero, or `false` was not treated as an absent field. A source-declared revision remained distinct from an inferred revision and its inference rule. Diagnostic pointers named their coordinate space and representation hash. Conflicting candidates remained an explicit set, so record order could not silently create unique identity.

The [boundary report](evidence/reader-boundary-report.json) contains 38 development calls: 26 counterexamples prepared alongside the revision and twelve regression cases from the earlier failures. All satisfied the declared checks, totaling 772 satisfied and zero violated criteria. A paired permutation control preserved ambiguity and the candidate-value hash set. The revised mapping and rubric differed from the earlier ones, so this was neither a held-out acceptance test nor an instrument-only retest. “Qualified” in the historical record means that these finite declared boundaries were checked; it does not establish correctness on arbitrary future packets.

## Targeted revision and a strong alternative

The [targeted revision report](evidence/targeted-revision-comparison.json) compares an explicit dependency graph with flat rows that contain the same material dependencies and conditions. Both use a shared evidence validator and serializer. The experimental difference is how affected records are selected. This is a deliberately strong simple alternative, not a form deprived of relevant information.

Ten distinct templates were instantiated for three books, producing thirty paired cases and sixty executions. All sixty produced the expected outcome; both representations agreed on all three book instances of every template. The shared validator and serializer restrict this comparison to the record-selection step and help explain equality once both selectors choose the same records. Thirty pairs are not thirty independently designed mechanisms.

| Template family | Paired book instances with equal outcomes |
| --- | ---: |
| No source or contract change | 3/3 |
| Valid representation map | 3/3 |
| Incorrect representation map | 3/3 |
| Missing representation map | 3/3 |
| Empty representation map | 3/3 |
| Changed source text | 3/3 |
| Changed image bytes | 3/3 |
| Required uncertainty preserved | 3/3 |
| Required uncertainty omitted | 3/3 |
| Required uncertainty unknown | 3/3 |

The outputs included 36 unresolved-mapping assessments and twelve unknown-contract assessments because those were declared outcomes of particular controls. These states are visible results, not failures concealed behind a pass total. The [addressability study source](evidence/addressability-study-source.json) describes the design and shared machinery; the machine comparison retains the per-template and per-book identities.

Source changes created separate candidate revisions and retained prior records and history. New assessments did not invent current-use authority. Timing was a single local invocation including shared decoding, not model latency or product cost. This comparison establishes no advantage for graph representation on the tested scope.

## Executable projection contract

The subsequent finite consumer accepts two representations of the same contract: nodes and typed edges, or rows with typed dependencies. It normalizes both and computes required closure from named roots. Relations distinguish content requirements, qualifications, and assessment requirements. Source attachments also distinguish content support from assessment basis. Evidence used to evaluate a card must not silently become the source from which the card's content was derived.

Typed identities carry record and revision identifiers, the contract of creation, and prior-version references. Assessments separately identify their exact target, assessment contract, and verdict. A request can include historical revisions in a separate history layer. Historical payloads and outgoing relations are preserved rather than edited to make an old record satisfy new rules.

Missing addresses, invalid sources, malformed input, or a closure exceeding the declared entity budget yield a blocked result with no usable partial output under this profile. An explicitly represented semantic unknown can remain useful content; it is not itself a schema failure. Minimality is relative to the declared relationships. The consumer cannot infer an omitted qualification merely because the author should have included it.

## Consumer qualification before the new responses

The [qualification report](evidence/consumer-qualification-report.json) records 51 control families in two forms: 102 executions, comprising 24 expected acceptances and 78 expected blocks. All matched, and normalized results agreed in all 51 pairs. A separately written verifier, which did not import the consumer, computed expected structural closure and rejected twelve deliberately damaged outputs. The verifier and consumer were developed within the same session and agent family. Separation of code paths is useful, but does not establish external independence or exclude shared assumptions.

Three pre-freeze corrections are disclosed. Invalid JSON types gained explicit guards; Python's equality between booleans and numbers could no longer mask a change in preserved JSON type; and normalized outputs explicitly retained relations and typed links. These changes preceded the new subject responses. The finite controls do not prove the consumer for every possible input or arbitrary non-JSON Python object.

## Five agent case pairs

The next stage fixed inputs, requests, reference material, rubric, implementations, and run order before obtaining ten responses in fresh agent contexts. There was one response per case and representation, with no corrective subject retry. The source-based tasks were deliberately related and comprise four source families, not ten independent samples.

| Case | Required preservation in a partial extraction | Observed result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Unreviewed navigation description | Useful description together with uncertain notation and missing context | Both main projections retained the uncertainty and source limit |
| Experimental cooking revision | A changed branch, its prior value, and a distinct independent branch | Prescribed current, historical, and separate-branch extractions retained their respective bindings |
| Changed assessment contract | Original creation contract and prior verdict, new assessment, and a new qualified revision | Old provenance remained intact; new assessment and revision were separate |
| Navigation table | Selected values, interpolation basis, sign, and unknown applicability | Both forms retained the specified values and grounds in all prescribed views |
| Electrical diagram image | Visible block and connection structure without invented numerical signals | Both forms retained qualitative observations and limits of numerical inference |

The [measured-results record](evidence/agent-projection-results.json) reports 24 available projections checked by the separately written verifier described above. Each received the rubric's highest fidelity and task-correctness score: both axes were saturated across all 24 projections. Differences smaller than one score step could not be resolved by that instrument. All prescribed views succeeded in five of five cases for each form, with equal primary outcomes in all five pairs. The ten raw responses were different; equal outcome scores do not imply identical representations or equal quality under a more discriminating assessment.

The semantic reviewer saw the representation, participated in design review, and belonged to the same agent family. No human expert review occurred. There were 84 nested critical-obligation judgments; these are not 84 independent experiments. Exact runtime model revision, decoding, seed, token use, latency, and compute cost were unavailable. Saved outputs were copied from visible final answers rather than captured as native API streams. Therefore the study supplies an observed agent case series, not a reproducible native-model benchmark.

## Counterexample to claim-level minimality

After evaluation, two row-form projections were found to include extra numerical statements inside a shared applicability qualification. An extraction of one selected table value consequently carried another value even though the latter's dedicated calculation node was not selected. The extra values were correct, and the frozen rubric allowed additional correct information. The original scores were retained; the observation was recorded separately.

This is a counterexample to inferring claim-level minimality from entity-level closure. A minimal reachable node set does not imply that every sentence inside those nodes is necessary, or that every incidental claim has all of its own grounds in the projection. A later contract could give individual claims addresses or constrain the scope of shared qualifications. That refinement is an implication of the counterexample, not a property already demonstrated by this run.

## Evidence and reproduction boundaries

The exact machine summaries and retained source views accompany this study; [origin.json](evidence/origin.json) distinguishes original JSON bytes from reconstructed text views. The [offline replay report](evidence/offline-projection-replay-report.json) records one historical extracted-bundle replay in which all 24 projections matched and passed a separate verifier. It explicitly reports no new subject-model calls, OCR, native trials, or semantic assessments. That replay is deterministic processing of saved outputs.

This public evidence folder does not contain the complete original source packages, candidate responses and frozen requests, or the complete native runtime. In particular, the frozen synthetic control bundle, original consumer, separate verifier and mutation artifacts are not supplied. Their absence is an unresolved recovery and publication gap for project-authored material, not a claim that book rights prohibit publishing synthetic controls or software. Their bound hashes and byte lengths are listed in the [recovery inventory](../historical-recovery-gaps.json); recreating similar code would produce a new experiment, not restore the historical one. Readers can inspect retained counts and limitations, but cannot infer a complete replay environment from hashes. The [public closure study](../dependency-closure-and-package-delivery/STUDY.md) provides a separately executable graph mechanism using metadata-only inputs.

The [projection study source](evidence/projection-study-source.json), addressability source and initial reader source are Russian prose views. The design, four failure mechanisms, status distribution and limitations above are English summaries of their relevant passages; they are not a full independently reviewed translation. The [evidence language index](../evidence-language-index.json) identifies every file. Agent-judging limitations are related to the [evaluation-method references](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#evaluation-methods); this local rubric does not reproduce their benchmarks.

## Conclusion

The studies demonstrate finite preservation of explicitly declared content, qualifications, assessment roles, and history through selected retrieval and revision operations. They also identify useful counterarguments: simple representations can reach the same outcomes, a corrected evaluator changes the interpretation of scores, and graph minimality does not bound the number of claims inside text. These results support the explicit obligations of the contract while keeping comparative effectiveness, arbitrary-root completeness, domain correctness, and product authorization outside the established conclusion. Definitions are consolidated in the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

# Controlled reuse: admission counterexamples and the cost of a cache hit

## Abstract

Reusing a prepared knowledge artifact can avoid work, but a matching identifier or a high hit rate does not establish that reuse is appropriate or economical. This analytical study develops explicit admission conditions and counterexamples involving missing dependencies, changed search scope, current authority, time boundaries, and overlapping cost claims. A homogeneous cost model gives a conditional break-even identity. Public code supplies worked arithmetic examples and a finite synthetic checker with all nine named gates, including missing-gate counterexamples. It does not report measured model savings, production throughput, or a deployed authorization system. The contribution is an inspectable set of obligations and counterexamples, with explicit boundaries between analytical consequences and empirical claims requiring observations.

## Question and objects

The question is when a retained artifact can be used for a new request without losing its requirements, overstating its evidence, or hiding the cost of validation. The scientific models are developed in [Controlled knowledge reuse](../../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md) and [Knowledge utility](../../../papers/knowledge-utility/PAPER.md). The [retained conditional-properties source](evidence/reuse-properties-source.json) records the original assumptions, arguments, and counterexamples behind this analysis.

A build can be represented as

\[
\operatorname{Compile}(b,q,S)\rightarrow(a,D,E,L),
\]

where \(b\) is a recipe, \(q\) the request, \(S\) the declared source snapshot, \(a\) the prepared revision, \(D\) its material dependencies, \(E\) evidence, and \(L\) coverage, loss, and stopping information. A later request \(q'\) requires a separate reuse decision under a current policy and context. Similarity may retrieve a candidate; it does not establish suitability.

Build dependencies explain how an artifact was obtained. Admission dependencies govern whether it can now be supplied or applied. A changed access decision can prohibit delivery without changing the artifact's text. A changed generator version need not require rebuilding every unaffected artifact. These decisions require explicit relevance rules rather than one undifferentiated freshness flag.

## Admission before optimization

The [status model](../../specifications/status-model/SPECIFICATION.md) distinguishes `pass`, `fail`, `unknown` and `not_applicable`. A mandatory obligation is discharged by `pass`, or by `not_applicable` together with an identified rule and evidence showing that exclusion is valid for the exact scope. Unsupported exclusion, missing observations and applicable unknowns do not discharge it. The nine gate classes used here are integrity, result class, task requirements, material dependency equivalence, current access, temporal policy, permitted transformations, modality delivery, and budget. The finite public checker deliberately makes all nine applicable; it therefore requires an explicit `pass` for every one and rejects `not_applicable` without a witness. It does not infer applicability from unrestricted text.

Partial content can be admissible when the request explicitly permits it and the gaps remain visible. That does not make the conditions attached to the selected content optional. High predicted utility also cannot turn a failed mandatory condition into permission. Economic optimization occurs within the set of admissible alternatives.

Reuse strategies include exact reuse, instantiation of a template, reuse after revalidation, rebuilding, bypassing the cache, and refusing reuse. These strategies are not a second operation-outcome vocabulary: the operation still reports `success`, `partial`, `refused`, `failed` or `unresolved` under its contract. A decision to revalidate is not itself permission to serve before the necessary check completes. An instantiated result has its own identity and must be checked after substitution.

## Counterexamples that determine the design

| Proposed shortcut | Counterexample | Required distinction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Listed dependencies are unchanged, so the result is preserved | An omitted legend changes the meaning of an unchanged number or symbol | Dependency completeness is a substantive premise, not a manifest label |
| A negative answer has an empty dependency set | A new matching item is added after an exhaustive search returned none | Absence, count, and exact top-k depend on search-domain membership and policy |
| A previously accepted payload can be sent to another requester | The requester, tenant, resource scope, or permission has changed | Content identity and current delivery authority are separate |
| A live cache lifetime overrides an unavailable authority service | A permission was revoked while the authority result became unknown | Unknown current authority does not inherit an old success by default |
| A cached completion message completes another task | The message describes an earlier attempt and external effect | Retained content is not a new execution event |
| Frequent reads prove continuing validity | Repeated hits add no source assessment | Access statistics and validation time are separate |

The preservation claim is necessarily conditional. Suppose a sound prior assessment established properties \(P_1,\ldots,P_m\) of artifact \(a\) for request \(q\) in state \(s_0\). Their complete dependency and interpretation interface must remain equivalent in \(s_1\), and each property's request dependence must factor through declared components on which \(q\) and the new request \(q'\) agree. For every output \(a'\) allowed by the transformation relation \(T(a,a')\), the transformation must preserve those properties. These premises establish their retention, but admission for \(q'\) additionally requires that the retained properties entail discharge of every mandatory requirement of \(q'\), with valid inapplicability witnesses where used, and that current authorization and delivery obligations hold. An old verified property set cannot justify an uncovered new requirement. Omitting an actual dependency or assuming that the old assessment was sound invalidates a premise; a Boolean field called `complete_manifest` establishes neither premise. The [controlled-reuse theorem](../../../papers/controlled-knowledge-reuse/PAPER.md) states the corresponding conditional argument.

Suitability is also directional. A complete nonempty set may support an existential answer through an allowed projection. One known member does not establish the whole set, its count, or absence of other members. A symmetric similarity score cannot supply this missing implication.

## Time, concurrency, and state

Artifact lifecycle, residency, freshness, and current eligibility are different assessments. A valid retained revision may be cold or temporarily unavailable. A resident fresh artifact may be inaccessible to this requester. A historical answer can deliberately use a pinned old revision while still respecting current access restrictions.

A strict-current policy needs a declared point at which relevant source heads and authority are considered current. A bounded-stale policy needs an explicit allowed scope and age. Neither policy may silently ignore known withdrawal, corruption, incompatibility, or a material unresolved gap. A cache hit updates usage statistics; it does not reset the time when an underlying source was checked. [HTTP cache freshness](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#http-caching) and [stale-response extensions](../../../reference/bibliography/BIBLIOGRAPHY.md#stale-http-responses) supply limited technical analogies, not permission to bypass knowledge or authority checks.

There is also a gap between checking and delivery. A permission or dependency can change after validation but before the result is supplied. An implementation must specify its consistency boundary, for example through a checked epoch, lease, or transactional publication protocol. Those mechanisms need their own concurrency tests. The analytical obligation here does not claim that a distributed service has already implemented them correctly.

## An exact cost model

Consider homogeneous requests with equal admissible quality and non-overlapping accounting categories over a fixed accounting horizon. Let \(N\) be the number of requests, \(G\) the cost of the cold admissible path, \(c_v\) additional candidate-search and admission cost per request, \(c_r\) the remaining cost of a usable reuse, and \(\rho\) the fraction of usable reuses. Let \(B\), \(MG\), \(I\), and \(S\) be preparation, additional background builds, invalidation, and storage costs. All costs use one declared unit and are nonnegative, with \(0\leq\rho\leq1\). The retained edition-1.0 examples used \(V,R,h\) for \(c_v,c_r,\rho\); their original data and code remain unchanged. Then

\[
C_0=NG,
\]

\[
C_1=B+Nc_v+N\rho c_r+N(1-\rho)G+MG+I+S.
\]

Writing \(F(N)=B+MG+I+S\) for overhead, which may depend on request count over the declared horizon, and \(d=\rho(G-c_r)-c_v\), subtraction gives

\[
C_0-C_1=Nd-F(N).
\]

Strict savings require \(Nd>F(N)\). The closed threshold \(\lfloor F/d\rfloor+1\) applies only when overhead \(F\), expected hit fraction \(\rho\), and unit costs are independent of \(N\) over that horizon, and \(d>0\). If \(d\leq0\), nonnegative overhead cannot be recovered through more requests in this homogeneous model. For actual observations, use the integer hit count \(H_N\): savings are \(H_N(G-c_r)-Nc_v-F(N)\). A fractional expected hit count is not an observed event count. If storage grows as \(S(N)=sN\), fixed other overhead \(F_0\) is recovered only when \(d>s\), with threshold \(\lfloor F_0/(d-s)\rfloor+1\); there is no such threshold when \(d\leq s\).

| Invented scenario | Parameters | Algebraic result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| High hit rate with expensive validation | \(N=100,\rho=0.9,G=10,c_r=1,c_v=9,F=0\) | Cold cost 1,000; reuse cost 1,090; loss 90 |
| Positive margin with preparation cost | \(N=100,\rho=0.9,G=10,c_r=1,c_v=1,F=100\) | Cold cost 1,000; reuse cost 390; savings 610 |
| Zero margin | \(N=100,\rho=0.5,G=10,c_r=2,c_v=4,F=100\) | The fixed loss of 100 remains |
| Storage increases with request count | Same positive-margin parameters, but \(F(N)=100+8N\) | At \(N=100\), reuse cost 1,190; loss 190; no profitable count under these constant rates |

These are exact arithmetic examples in invented cost units. They are not prices, measured token bills, or estimates of the project's performance. Heterogeneous requests require an appropriately stratified sum rather than substitution of incompatible averages.

## Utility and double counting

Compute savings alone are not net task value. Quality loss, additional review, delayed outcomes, and downstream harm require an explicit utility policy and evidence. If their values are unknown, assigning zero would create an unsupported benefit. If a route violates a hard constraint, a favorable monetary estimate does not admit it.

Costs also need a shared baseline. Suppose a cold operation costs ten units, provider caching reduces it to six, and using a prepared artifact reduces it to one. The two separately calculated savings, four and nine, cannot be added into thirteen units saved from a ten-unit operation. If the artifact hit eliminated the model call, a provider-cache saving on that nonexistent call is not another observed benefit. Resource events, retries, storage, and shared infrastructure need non-overlapping accounting or an explicit allocation rule.

No inference about causal contribution follows merely from counting citations, delivered artifacts, or completed tasks. The [editorial pilot](../editorial-skill-pilot/STUDY.md), for example, observed usable outputs under both conditions and a tie. That result cannot be turned into measured economic benefit without resource and outcome observations.

## Public examples and claim limits

The corrected [counterexample program](checks/check_admission_and_cost.py) names all nine mandatory gate classes. It checks a fully passing positive control and 27 separate missing, failed or unknown gate mutations, plus malformed inputs including a one-gate-only map and an unjustified `not_applicable`. The supplied states remain local inputs; this does not authenticate an authority service or establish real-world applicability. The exact rational cost calculations are worked examples of the identity, including linearly growing storage overhead, not empirical validation of economic assumptions. The small dependency and absence models illustrate counterexamples on invented inputs.

```bash
python3 checks/check_admission_and_cost.py
```

The [saved corrected result](checks/admission-and-cost-check.json) is a new deterministic demonstration. The earlier [edition-1.0 program](evidence/replay_reuse_counterexamples.py) and [result](evidence/public-reuse-counterexamples.json) remain byte-identical as historical evidence: that program tested only three supplied gates and erroneously accepted a nonempty subset when a required gate was omitted. Its six fail/unknown controls do not establish the missing-gate obligation. The corrected program supersedes that local gate demonstration without changing any historical model outcome. The [correction record](../review-corrections.json) identifies the new files separately.

The original property source is a Russian rendered-text view with separately recorded source and representation hashes in [origin.json](evidence/origin.json). The admission conditions, dependency counterexamples and temporal limits above are English summaries and the current argument; the strengthened new-request-coverage premise is an explicit correction, not a claim that the old source already proved it. The [evidence language index](../evidence-language-index.json) records individual file languages. None of the public programs reruns an earlier production system.

## Conclusion

Controlled reuse requires two decisions: whether a route satisfies its obligations, and whether it is preferable among admissible routes. The logical counterexamples identify inputs that cannot be omitted from the first decision. The cost identity specifies what must be measured for the second. The completed analytical result is useful precisely because it permits a negative answer: a cache can be correct but uneconomical, economical under invented assumptions but unmeasured in practice, or superficially efficient while inadmissible. Terminology is consolidated in the [glossary](../../../reference/glossary/GLOSSARY.md).

BLOB engineering/studies/source-transcription-and-uncertainty/STUDY.md 6119790ada666b76782c0952e12e47799c51893a85f71a7e36f72d9ac44f3fe0 original 14084

title: "Reproducible transcription is not preservation of uncertainty"
artifact: "engineering-study"
edition: "1.1"
author: "Pavel Valentov"
